## PMID 35259149

Wijesinghe A (2022) Conjugates for use in peptide therapeutics: A systematic review and meta-analysis. *PLoS One*.
DOI: https://doi.org/10.1371/journal.pone.0255753

- Contributes **32 records** (30 distinct, the rest are exact repeats), 7 unique sequences; 32 carry a numeric half-life.
- Recovered design: in vivo PK; PEPlife2 `Vivo/Vitro`: in vivo.
- Live animal dosed in 32 of 32 records.
- Assay matrices: plasma|serum.
- Evidence available: **curated extraction** (PMC PMC8903268).
- Reported half-life: 2.2-33.6 h (median 7 h).
- PEPlife2 dose route: IV|SC.
- Curated extraction: **file present but empty** - re-extract.
- Bioanalytical method: not applicable (no primary bioanalysis performed by the authors).
- Caveats: This paper is a PRISMA systematic review and meta-analysis (PROSPERO CRD42020222579), not a primary study. No animals, humans, tissues, or cells were dosed or tested by these authors, so no study_types, in_vivo_species, or agents could be assigned from the body. All half-life values in the body (Table 3, Table 4, Fig 2) are secondary data transcribed from 16 other primary studies (e.g. Fukushima 2019 insulin-chondroitin/heparosan conjugates, Ichikawa 2018 GLP-1C-chondroitin/heparosan conjugates, which are the studies corresponding to the peplife2 rows for this PMID). Per the protocol's no-inference rule, these third-party values were not recorded as agents of this paper; the original publications should be extracted instead. The body does describe, in aggregate, the animal models used by the included studies ("varying strains of mice (BALB, ICR, and CD-1), rats (SD, Wistar), and monkeys"), but no species was dosed in this work. Eligibility was restricted to intravenously administered peptides smaller than insulin (5.8 kDa) composed of L-amino acids. The bioanalytical_quote above describes the methods used by the included primary studies, not by this review. Note also that the body reports a PMID/journal-agnostic full text; the header block PMID field was blank in the source file.

## PMID 35177945

Palmer ES (2022) Potential Therapeutic Role for Apelin and Related Peptides in Diabetes: An Update. *Clin Med Insights Endocrinol Diabetes*.
DOI: https://doi.org/10.1177/11795514221074679

- Contributes **16 records**, 12 unique sequences; 16 carry a numeric half-life.
- Recovered design: ex vivo (fluid stability)|in vivo PK; PEPlife2 `Vivo/Vitro`: in vitro|in vivo.
- Live animal dosed in 1 of 16 records.
- Assay matrices: plasma.
- Evidence available: **curated extraction** (PMC PMC8844737).
- Reported half-life: 0.0767-24 h (median 10.1 h).
- PEPlife2 dose route: IP|N.A..
- Curated extraction: study types review; 0 agents, 0 with a body-quoted half-life.
- Bioanalytical method: not reported.
- Caveats: CONFIRMED NARRATIVE REVIEW, NOT A PRIMARY STUDY. The body generates no new data: it has no Methods section, no animal ethics statement (instead 'There are no ethical issues related to this review article'), and every experimental statement is attributed to other laboratories. Per instruction, no PK values were harvested. For transparency about what was deliberately NOT extracted: the body cites, from other papers, an in vitro half-life for acylated apelin-13 analogues of '24 hours vs >2.1 hours' for native apelin-13, and states that 'Lys GluPAL(Tyr )apelin-13 had an in vivo half-life of 2.5 to 3 hours'; it also mentions qualitatively that pGlu-apelin-13 'has a longer half-life' than apelin-13 and that native apelin-13 has a 'short biological half-life'. These are third-party findings reported by the review, not findings of this article, so they were not entered as agent-level PK. Likewise the many doses quoted in the body (e.g. 25 nmol/kg bw apelin-13 analogues, 2 mg/kg (pGlu)apelin-13 osmotic pump, 9 or 30 nmol/kg bw in 16 overweight men) all belong to cited studies, so no agent objects were created. study_types is recorded as ['review'] because none of the protocol's clinical / in vivo / ex vivo / in vitro categories describes work performed by these authors. Table 1 does print apelin isoform sequences (e.g. apelin-13 QRPRLSHKGPMPF, apelin-17 KFRRQRPRLSHKGPMPF), which matches the PEPLIFE2 metadata peptides, but as the review dosed nothing these were not turned into agent entries.

## PMID 36631971

Mohammed GK (2023) In Vitro Properties and Pharmacokinetics of Temporarily PEGylated Onc72 Prodrugs. *Adv Healthc Mater*.
DOI: https://doi.org/10.1002/adhm.202202368

- Contributes **12 records** (5 distinct, the rest are exact repeats), 2 unique sequences; 12 carry a numeric half-life.
- Recovered design: in vivo PK; PEPlife2 `Vivo/Vitro`: in vivo.
- Live animal dosed in 12 of 12 records.
- Assay matrices: plasma|serum.
- Evidence available: **curated extraction** (PMC PMC11469207).
- Reported half-life: 0.717-14 h (median 1.1 h).
- PEPlife2 dose route: N.A.|SC.
- Curated extraction: study types in vivo|ex vivo|in vitro; 9 agents, 3 with a body-quoted half-life.
- Curated in vivo routes: not_administered|sc.
- Curated ex vivo matrices: blood|not_reported|plasma|serum.
- Bioanalytical method: Competitive ELISA (cELISA) using protein-G-captured anti-Onc72 monoclonal antibody 4H6 with carboxyfluorescein-Onc72 reporter (fluorescence, lambda-exc 485 nm / lambda-em 535 nm) for total Onc72; plus LC-MS (ACQUITY UPLC M-Class coupled to ESI-QTOF Synapt G2-Si, scheduled multiple reaction monitoring) with 13C,15N-labeled Onc72 internal standard for free Onc72 and metabolites; RP-HPLC/ESI-IT-MS for serum and lysate release experiments; HDMSE for urine metabolite identification.
- Body-quoted half-lives: Onc72: 43 min (elimination t1/2, Table 1; text states "an elimination half-life of ~43 min") [CD-1 mouse plasma (K2EDTA) after subcutaneous administration] ;; 5k-prodrug (5 kDa PEG-Leu-Val-Pro-Arg-Onc72): 66 min (elimination t1/2 in mouse plasma, cELISA total Onc72); 8 h for Onc72 release in mouse serum ex vivo [CD-1 mouse plasma (in vivo elimination); mouse serum (ex vivo release half-life)] ;; 20k-prodrug (20 kDa PEG-Leu-Val-Pro-Arg-Onc72): 324 min (Table 1); text reports "prolonged elimination t1/2 of 66 min and even 5.5 h"; 14 h for Onc72 release in mouse serum ex vivo [CD-1 mouse plasma (in vivo elimination); mouse serum (ex vivo release half-life)].
- Caveats: Elimination half-lives are for total Onc72-immunoreactive material (prodrug + released Onc72 + epitope-containing metabolites) measured by cELISA; Table 1 gives t1/2 43, 66 and 324 min for Onc72, 5k-prodrug and 20k-prodrug respectively, while the body text describes the 20k-prodrug value as '~5.5 h'. The ex vivo mouse-serum release half-lives (8 h for 5k-prodrug, 14 h for 20k-prodrug) come from Figure 1 and are release, not elimination, half-lives. Additional Table 1 parameters: V 47.3 / 115.3 / 171.8 mL, AUC0-inf 5.5 / 12.7 / 139 µg x h/mL, CL 0.76 / 1.22 / 0.35 mL min-1, MRT 53 / 111 / 524 min. The body states Onc72 has a reported mouse-serum half-life of ~3 h and an intraperitoneal elimination t1/2 of ~14 min, but both are explicitly attributed to earlier publications and were therefore not extracted. Mice were grouped by blood-sampling timepoint (groups 1-3) rather than by a fixed n per arm. LOD/LOQ: 4.5 and 9 µg L-1 for Onc72; cELISA urine LOQ 46 µg L-1. Lower-detail agents also present in the body but not separately listed: human alpha-thrombin (enzymatic release reagent), Cf-Onc72 reporter, mAb 4H6, recombinant protein G, casein.

## PMID 30504081

Banskota S (2019) Long circulating genetically encoded intrinsically disordered zwitterionic polypeptides for drug delivery. *Biomaterials*.
DOI: https://doi.org/10.1016/j.biomaterials.2018.11.012

- Contributes **10 records**, 5 unique sequences; 10 carry a numeric half-life.
- Recovered design: in vivo PK; PEPlife2 `Vivo/Vitro`: in vivo.
- Live animal dosed in 10 of 10 records.
- Assay matrices: plasma.
- Evidence available: **curated extraction** (PMC PMC6376874).
- Reported half-life: 4.5-15.6 h (median 10.7 h).
- PEPlife2 dose route: IV|SC.
- Curated extraction: study types in vivo|in vitro; 13 agents, 7 with a body-quoted half-life.
- Curated in vivo routes: iv|other|sc.
- Bioanalytical method: Fluorescence quantification of Alexa488-labelled polypeptide in whole blood against an Alexa488 standard curve; 125I gamma counting (automated gamma counter) for the biodistribution arm.
- Body-quoted half-lives: (VPGAG)120 (ELP120, repeat-unit-matched uncharged control): 4.5 ± 0.2 h (intravenous) [mouse blood/plasma (Alexa488 fluorescence in blood collected into heparin)] ;; (VPGAG)160 (ELP160, molecular-weight-matched uncharged control): 6.6 ± 0.3 h (intravenous) [mouse blood/plasma (Alexa488 fluorescence in blood collected into heparin)] ;; (VPKEG)120 (ZIPP, optimal zwitterionic polypeptide): 12.0 ± 0.4 h (intravenous) [mouse blood/plasma (Alexa488 fluorescence in blood collected into heparin)] ;; (VPREG)120 (ZIPP with arginine/glutamate): 9.6 ± 0.5 h (intravenous) [mouse blood/plasma (Alexa488 fluorescence in blood collected into heparin)] ;; (VPKDG)120 (ZIPP with lysine/aspartate): 10.8 ± 0.2 h (intravenous) [mouse blood/plasma (Alexa488 fluorescence in blood collected into heparin)] ;; (VPRDG)120 (ZIPP with arginine/aspartate): 8.2 ± 0.3 h (intravenous) [mouse blood/plasma (Alexa488 fluorescence in blood collected into heparin)] ;; (VPKEG)80 (shorter ZIPP, 40 kDa): 12.5 h (intravenous) [mouse blood/plasma (Alexa488 fluorescence in blood collected into heparin)].
- Caveats: Half-lives in Tables 2 and 3 are explicitly elimination half-lives (t1/2β): METHODS states 'The elimination half-life (t1/2β) was calculated from the slope of the linear regression fit to the elimination portion of the log concentration vs. time curve', and the RESULTS text names them 'elimination half-life'. The column header is t1/2 (h) - a time, not a rate constant - so no ln2/k conversion was needed or performed. No absorption half-life is reported anywhere in the body; tmax (s.c. only, Table 3) is the only absorption-related parameter and was NOT used to derive an absorption half-life. Cmax is defined in METHODS ('tmax for s.c. PK data was determined as the time required to reach Cmax') but no numeric Cmax value is printed in the body. AUC for the (VPKEG)80/(VPGAG)80 length series is deferred to Table S1 (supplementary) and so is recorded as not reported in body. PEG-48kDa (14 h) and XTEN432 (9 h) half-lives quoted in the Discussion are cited literature values for substances NOT administered in this study, so they are not listed as agents. GLP1 fusion arms have efficacy (blood glucose, body weight) but no drug-concentration PK. Mouse and human serum albumin were binding partners in a native-PAGE assay rather than administered agents, so they are not listed as agents; because purified albumin protein was used rather than serum, that assay is classified in vitro and no ex vivo work is present.

- Reported half-life: 0.81-10.1 h (median 4.26 h).
- PEPlife2 dose route: SC.
- Curated extraction: study types in vivo|in vitro; 9 agents, 8 with a body-quoted half-life.
- Curated in vivo routes: sc.
- Bioanalytical method: Non-invasive in vivo fluorescence imaging (IVIS Spectrum, Perkin Elmer; ex 660 nm / em 680 nm) of sulfo-Cy5-labelled peptide, quantified as radiant efficiency in regions of interest with LivingImage software.
- Body-quoted half-lives: FUD (Functional Upstream Domain peptide, also called pUR4); dosed as unlabelled FUD spiked with 1.5% FUD-sulfo-Cy5: 0.81 h (apparent absorption t1/2; reported as 3.3 h, 6.8 h, 10.1 h in the running text for the 10/20/40 kDa conjugates) [mouse subcutaneous injection site (apparent half-life of loss of fluorescence signal from the s.c. depot, i.e. absorption half-life, not plasma elimination)] ;; 10 kDa PEG-FUD: 3.28 h (Table 2); stated as 3.3 h in the text [mouse subcutaneous injection site (apparent absorption half-life by IVIS fluorescence)] ;; 20 kDa PEG-FUD: 6.77 h (Table 2); stated as 6.8 h in the text [mouse subcutaneous injection site (apparent absorption half-life by IVIS fluorescence)] ;; 40 kDa PEG-FUD: 10.09 h (Table 2); stated as 10.1 h in the text [mouse subcutaneous injection site (apparent absorption half-life by IVIS fluorescence)] ;; mFUD (mutated FUD peptide, no fibronectin activity): 0.86 h (Table 2) [mouse subcutaneous injection site (apparent absorption half-life by IVIS fluorescence)] ;; 10 kDa PEG-mFUD: 3.06 h (Table 2); stated as 3.1 h in the text [mouse subcutaneous injection site (apparent absorption half-life by IVIS fluorescence)] ;; 20 kDa PEG-mFUD: 5.25 h (Table 2); stated as 5.3 h in the text [mouse subcutaneous injection site (apparent absorption half-life by IVIS fluorescence)] ;; 40 kDa PEG-mFUD: 8.36 h (Table 2); stated as 8.4 h in the text [mouse subcutaneous injection site (apparent absorption half-life by IVIS fluorescence)].
- Caveats: All reported half-lives are APPARENT ABSORPTION half-lives (50% loss of fluorescence signal from the subcutaneous depot), not plasma elimination half-lives; no plasma or serum concentrations were measured in this study, so cmax and tmax are absent throughout. Half-lives were derived from a one-phase decay fit applied to different terminal windows for each peptide (0-48 h for FUD/mFUD, 1-48 h for 10 kDa, 3-48 h for 20 kDa and 6-48 h for 40 kDa conjugates) because larger drugs showed an initial signal-reduction lag. No amino acid sequence is given anywhere in the body for FUD or mFUD; the sequences shown in the peplife2 header block are database metadata and were not used. Kidney and bladder fluorescence localisation over time is described qualitatively (Fig. 10, Fig. 11) without quantified concentrations. ITC binding constants (Table 1) are in vitro affinity data, not PK. Vehicle for all doses was PBS pH 7.4, but PBS alone was not dosed as a separate control arm, so it is not listed as an agent.

Yang G (2022) Palmitic Acid-Conjugated Radiopharmaceutical for Integrin αvβ3-Targeted Radionuclide Therapy. *Pharmaceutics*.
DOI: https://doi.org/10.3390/pharmaceutics14071327

- Contributes **8 records**, 2 unique sequences; 8 carry a numeric half-life.
- Recovered design: in vivo PK; PEPlife2 `Vivo/Vitro`: in vivo.
- Live animal dosed in 8 of 8 records.
- Assay matrices: blood.
- Evidence available: **curated extraction** (PMC PMC9321335).
- Reported half-life: 0.0177-1.22 h (median 0.136 h).
- PEPlife2 dose route: Injection|injection.
- Curated extraction: study types in vivo|in vitro; 5 agents, 3 with a body-quoted half-life.
- Curated in vivo routes: iv.
- Bioanalytical method: Gamma counting of 177Lu radioactivity in weighed blood, tissue and cell samples with a gamma-counter, expressed as %IA/g; radio-HPLC (Agilent 1260 with radioactive detector, C18 column) for radiochemical purity and for in vivo stability in urine; small-animal SPECT/CT (Mediso) for imaging.
- Body-quoted half-lives: 177Lu-Palm-3PRGD2 ([177Lu]Lu-DOTA-Palm-3PRGD2): 73.42 min (T1/2beta, KM mice); 63.71 min (T1/2beta, C57BL/6 mice) [mouse blood (percentage injected activity per gram, %IA/g)] ;; 177Lu-3PRGD2 ([177Lu]Lu-DOTA-3PRGD2): 11.81 min (T1/2beta, KM mice); 14.54 min (T1/2beta, C57BL/6 mice) [mouse blood (%IA/g)] ;; 177Lu-Palm ([177Lu]Lu-DOTA-Palm, albumin binder without targeting peptide): 29.82 min (T1/2beta, KM mice) [mouse blood (%IA/g)].
- Caveats: The header line of the source file reads 'PMID: ?'; the PMID field is set to the assigned value 35890224. No Cmax or tmax is reported for any agent: dosing was intravenous and blood data are expressed as %IA/g versus time without a reported peak parameter. No absorption half-life and no rate constant is reported anywhere, so nothing was converted. The body reports a two-phase decay fit with half-lives labelled T1/2alpha and T1/2beta but never uses the words 'distribution' or 'elimination' for those phases; half_life_phase is therefore recorded as terminal/beta (the literal label) and half_life_elimination carries the T1/2beta value with an explicit caveat. Alpha-phase values (177Lu-Palm-3PRGD2 4.49 min, 177Lu-3PRGD2 1.94 min, 177Lu-Palm 3.33 min in KM mice; 2.08 and 1.06 min in C57BL/6 mice) are deliberately kept out of the headline half_life field. Blood clearance was measured in normal female KM mice while biodistribution and therapy used female C57BL/6 MC38 tumour-bearing mice; the Discussion flags this strain mismatch as a study limitation. Human serum albumin was added to one in vitro competition binding condition (IC50 5.82 ± 1.29 nM with HSA) but was not dosed and is not listed as an agent. The therapy control is described as 'saline' in Methods 2.11 but as 'PBS (as a control)' in the Figure 5 legend; both wordings are recorded. The effective absorbed human dose of 4.04 x 10-2 mSv/MBq is an OLINDA dosimetry estimate extrapolated from mouse biodistribution, not a measured PK parameter.

## PMID 28575099

Böttger R (2017) Differential stability of therapeutic peptides with different proteolytic cleavage sites in blood, plasma and serum. *PLoS One*.
DOI: https://doi.org/10.1371/journal.pone.0178943

- Contributes **8 records**, 6 unique sequences; 0 carry a numeric half-life.
- Recovered design: ex vivo (fluid stability); PEPlife2 `Vivo/Vitro`: in vitro.
- Live animal dosed in 0 of 8 records.
- Assay matrices: plasma.
- Evidence available: **curated extraction** (PMC PMC5456363).
- PEPlife2 dose route: N.A..
- Curated extraction: study types ex vivo|in vitro; 8 agents, 6 with a body-quoted half-life.
- Curated ex vivo matrices: blood|not_reported|plasma|serum.
- Bioanalytical method: RP-HPLC with UV detection at 214 nm (Jupiter C18) for quantification of intact peptide relative to time zero; LC coupled on-line to electrospray-ionization ion-trap MS (Agilent 1100 / Bruker Esquire HCT) for metabolite identification and quantification; MALDI-TOF/TOF-MS for monoisotopic mass confirmation of synthesized peptides.
- Body-quoted half-lives: Api88: around 1 hour in fresh mouse blood (this study); less than 5 min in mouse serum (cited prior work) [fresh CD-1 mouse blood (ex vivo); mouse serum for the cited <5 min value] ;; Api134: 4 h in mouse serum (cited prior work); no new half-life value determined here [mouse serum] ;; Api137: 6 h in mouse serum (cited prior work); no new half-life value determined here [mouse serum] ;; Onc18: 20 min in serum per Results; 25 min in mouse serum per Discussion (both cited prior work); no new half-life value determined here [mouse serum] ;; Onc72: 3 h in mouse serum (cited prior work); no new half-life value determined here [mouse serum] ;; Onc112: more than 8 h in mouse serum (cited prior work); no new half-life value determined here [mouse serum].
- Caveats: This study contains NO in vivo dosing. Mice were euthanized and terminally bled by cardiac puncture into a syringe that already contained the peptide solution, so all peptide exposure is ex vivo. in_vivo_species is therefore an empty array. The PeplLife2 metadata labels these rows 'In Vitro'; per the protocol's definition, blood/serum/plasma degradation assays are ex vivo (the human alpha-thrombin digestion in S3 Fig is the only genuinely in vitro exposure). None of the eight peptides had a half-life measured in this study; the qualitative PeplLife2 descriptors (Highly/Medium/Low Stable) correspond to Table 3 'Stability of eight peptides in fresh blood, serum, and plasma' (High: Api134/Api137/Onc112; Medium: Onc18/Onc72; Low: Api88/AAYR-Onc112/LVPR-Onc112). The numeric half-lives quoted in the body (Api88 <5 min, Api134 4 h, Api137 6 h, Onc18 20 min in Results but 25 min in Discussion, Onc72 3 h, Onc112 >8 h, all in mouse serum) are explicitly attributed to earlier publications, not measured here; note the internal 20 vs 25 min discrepancy for Onc18. The statement that 'Onc112 exhibited an improved pharmacokinetic profile and around fivefold higher plasma levels than Onc72 after intravenous application in mice' is likewise cited prior work. Peptide concentration in every matrix was ~31.5 umol/L; blood volume drawn into the peptide syringe was 200-300 uL. Sampling: 1 and 60 min in blood (1, 10 and 30 min for Api88); 0 and 60 min in serum/plasma (0, 10 and 30 min for Api88). Replication: 'at least in triplicates' for stability tests, and 'Six to nine samples were analyzed for each matrix' for the fibrinopeptide A analysis. Api137 was used as an internal 100%-stability reference to bound a ~10% matrix-effect error in the blood samples.

## PMID 31156041

Griffiths K (2019) Half-life extension and non-human primate pharmacokinetic safety studies of i-body AD-114 targeting human CXCR4. *MAbs*.
DOI: https://doi.org/10.1080/19420862.2019.1626652

- Contributes **7 records**, 1 unique sequences; 7 carry a numeric half-life.
- Recovered design: in vivo PK; PEPlife2 `Vivo/Vitro`: in vivo.
- Live animal dosed in 7 of 7 records.
- Assay matrices: plasma.
- Evidence available: **curated extraction** (PMC PMC6748587).
- Reported half-life: 0.18-19.2 h (median 7.77 h).
- PEPlife2 dose route: IP|IV|SC.
- Curated extraction: study types in vivo|in vitro; 7 agents, 6 with a body-quoted half-life.
- Curated in vivo routes: ip|iv|not_administered|sc.
- Bioanalytical method: LC-MS/MS monitoring the signature framework peptide LTPNQQR (XBridge BEH300 C18 50 x 2.1 mm 3.5 um reverse phase column with an AB Sciex QTRAP5500 triple quadrupole); ELISA for AD-114-PA600-6H in mouse plasma using anti-human NCAM-1 capture and anti-Histidine-HRP detection.
- Body-quoted half-lives: AD-114-Im7-FH (unmodified i-body fusion): 0.18 h [mouse plasma] ;; AD-114-Im7-FH-SA21 (albumin-binding peptide fusion): 0.95 h [mouse plasma] ;; AD-114-Im7-FH PEG 30K (linear 30 kDa PEG conjugate): 11.85 h [mouse plasma] ;; AD-114-Im7-FH PEG 2 x 20K (branched PEG conjugate): 19.24 h [mouse plasma] ;; AD-114-PA600-6H (PASylated i-body with C-terminal His6): 7.77 h (mouse, IV, 10 mg/kg) [mouse plasma] ;; AD-114-PA600 (PASylated i-body without His6 tag): 9.5 h (rat, IV, 3.5 mg/kg) [rat plasma].
- Caveats: All half-lives are non-compartmental terminal elimination half-lives: Methods define 't1/2 (terminal elimination half-life); Cmax (the maximum plasma concentration), tmax (time to maximum plasma concentration)'. No absorption half-life and no rate constant (ka/ke) is reported anywhere in the body, so half_life_absorption is 'not reported' for every agent even for the IP, SC and oral-equivalent routes where an absorption phase clearly exists. The paper notes for monkey SC dosing that 'the slower absorption resulted in a lower Cmax, as expected, and a longer tmax (6 h)', which is an absorption observation but not an absorption half-life. Mouse Cmax and tmax ARE reported in Table 1 for every variant and are captured above. Table 1 footnote b states error on the rodent PK values could not be determined because each animal was not bled at every timepoint (sparse sampling), so mouse and rat values carry no SD. Footnote c explains that rat SC t1/2 was 'NC' (not calculable) for want of three terminal-phase timepoints. The full AD-114 amino-acid sequence appears only in the PEPLIFE2 metadata header, which is database metadata and not an evidence source, so every sequence field is recorded as not reported in body. The predicted human terminal half-life of 35 h is a model simulation, not measured data, and was not entered as a PK value. Plasma handling (K2-EDTA, -80 C storage in Protein LoBind tubes) is bioanalytical sample processing, not an ex vivo treatment study, so ex vivo is not listed among study_types.

## PMID 35046019

Vanderschuren K (2022) Tuning protein half-life in mouse using sequence-defined biopolymers functionalized with lipids. *Proc Natl Acad Sci U S A*.
DOI: https://doi.org/10.1073/pnas.2103099119

- Contributes **7 records**, 3 unique sequences; 7 carry a numeric half-life.
- Recovered design: in vivo PK; PEPlife2 `Vivo/Vitro`: in vivo.
- Live animal dosed in 7 of 7 records.
- Assay matrices: plasma.
- Evidence available: **curated extraction** (PMC PMC8794819).
- Reported half-life: 1.6-33.3 h (median 19.6 h).
- PEPlife2 dose route: IV.
- Curated extraction: study types in vivo|in vitro; 7 agents, 3 with a body-quoted half-life.
- Curated in vivo routes: iv|not_administered|other|sc.
- Bioanalytical method: GFP-specific sandwich ELISA (GFP ELISA Kit, Abcam catalog #ab171581) on diluted tail-puncture blood.
- Body-quoted half-lives: ELP(0FA)-GFP: 1.7 h [C57BL/6J mouse blood/serum] ;; ELP(5FA)-GFP (untreated and ISAz-treated): 28 to 33 h (reported as a range shared with ELP(10FA)-GFP; construct-specific value is in SI Appendix, Table S1) [C57BL/6J mouse blood/serum] ;; ELP(10FA)-GFP (untreated and ISAz-treated): 28 to 33 h (up to 33 h, ~94% of the 35 h half-life of mouse serum albumin) [C57BL/6J mouse blood/serum].
- Caveats: The body reports only two explicit half-life numbers: 1.7 h for ELP(0FA)-GFP and a 28-33 h range shared by ISAz-treated ELP(5FA)-GFP, ISAz-treated ELP(10FA)-GFP and untreated ELP(10FA)-GFP. The seven construct-specific values in the PeplLife2 metadata (33.3, 31.7, 27.9, 1.6, 1.9, 19.6, 18.5 h) are NOT printed in the body; they are in SI Appendix, Table S1, which is not part of this file. Similarly, the full one-letter construct sequences listed in the metadata do not appear in the body - only the 15-residue ELP repeat VPGAGVPGXGVPGGG (X = Tyr or pAzF) is given, and note that this repeat is written in a different register than the metadata sequences. Dose is stated in two equivalent ways ('50 ug of each protein variant (10 uM in PBS)' in Results; '120 uL of 10 uM ELP-GFP' in Methods). Blood sampling schedule: 1, 4, 8, 16 and 24 h then daily for 7 d. Both intravenous and subcutaneous routes were used; the body states subcutaneous dosing gave a delayed peak concentration but equivalent half-lives, with supporting data only in the SI. Tmax and Cmax values are never reported numerically. Albumin half-lives quoted in the body for reference (MSA 35 h in mice; HSA ~19 d in humans) are literature/model values, not measurements made here. Organs (brain, lungs, heart, spleen, liver, kidneys) and serum were collected from dosed mice for Alexa Fluor 647 imaging and cytokine quantification; this is terminal sampling from an in vivo study rather than an ex vivo treatment assay, so no ex vivo study type was assigned.

## PMID 38340726

Bi W (2024) An enhanced broad-spectrum peptide inhibits Omicron variants in vivo. *Cell Rep Med*.
DOI: https://doi.org/10.1016/j.xcrm.2024.101418

- Contributes **5 records**, 1 unique sequences; 5 carry a numeric half-life.
- Recovered design: ex vivo (tissue homogenate)|in vivo PK; PEPlife2 `Vivo/Vitro`: in vivo.
- Live animal dosed in 5 of 5 records.
- Assay matrices: serum|tissue / homogenate.
- Evidence available: **curated extraction** (PMC PMC10897629).
- Reported half-life: 2.64-89.8 h (median 49 h).
- PEPlife2 dose route: Intranasally.
- Curated extraction: study types in vivo|ex vivo|in vitro; 10 agents, 2 with a body-quoted half-life.
- Curated in vivo routes: iv|not_administered|other.
- Curated ex vivo matrices: not_reported|plasma|serum|tissue_organ.
- Bioanalytical method: Indirect functional bioassay: serial dilution of mouse plasma or tissue homogenate followed by a SARS-CoV-2 D614G pseudovirus infection inhibition assay in Caco-2 cells, with the dilution fold achieving 50% inhibition (DF-IC50) converted to an estimated peptide concentration; pharmacokinetic parameters then computed with PKSolver software. No LC-MS/MS, ELISA or radiolabel assay is described..
- Body-quoted half-lives: A1L35HR2m-Chol (cholesterol-conjugated ACE2-HR2 fusion lipopeptide): approximately 81.8 h in plasma [mouse plasma (also lung, nose and trachea homogenates)] ;; A1L35HR2m (unmodified ACE2-HR2 fusion peptide): 2.6 h in plasma [mouse plasma].
- Caveats: Dosing for the PK study was intranasal, so an absorption phase exists, but the paper reports no absorption half-life and no absorption rate constant; half_life_absorption is therefore 'not reported' for all agents and nothing was converted. The half-lives were obtained by fitting estimated concentration-time data in PKSolver and no phase is named anywhere, so half_life_phase is 'unspecified' and half_life_elimination is left as not reported rather than inferred from an unnamed t1/2. A tmax of 8 h IS reported for A1L35HR2m-Chol in plasma, lung, nose and trachea ('The peptide concentration in the lung, nose, and trachea peaked at 8 h'), but no numeric Cmax is given in the body: the peak is expressed as a mean dilution fold (10,284-fold plasma, 58,770-fold lung, 18,231-fold nose, 1,959-fold trachea) with the estimated concentrations plotted only in Figures 5B and 5D. Tissue half-lives (lung 89.8 h, nose 49.0 h, trachea 10.6 h) are recorded in notes_agent alongside the headline plasma value of 81.8 h. Full amino-acid sequences appear only inside the Figure 1A image and in the PEPLIFE2 metadata header, so all sequence fields are recorded as not reported in body. The peptide concentration measurement is an indirect functional estimate rather than a direct analytical quantitation, which is a caveat on all reported concentrations and AUCs. Live SARS-CoV-2 isolates came from patient specimens, but no human was dosed, so the study is not classified as clinical. The virus challenge inocula (Omicron BA.5 and EG.5.1 at 10,000 PFU, SARS-CoV-2 Alpha at 200 PFU) are model-induction agents and are noted here rather than listed as agent objects.

## PMID 32603666

Thompson BR (2020) Pharmacokinetics of gemcitabine and its amino acid ester prodrug following intravenous and oral administrations in mice. *Biochem Pharmacol*.
DOI: https://doi.org/10.1016/j.bcp.2020.114127

- Contributes **5 records**, 1 unique sequences; 5 carry a numeric half-life.
- Recovered design: in vivo PK; PEPlife2 `Vivo/Vitro`: in vivo.
- Live animal dosed in 5 of 5 records.
- Assay matrices: plasma.
- Evidence available: **curated extraction** (PMC PMC7606647).
- Reported half-life: 0.0617-3.6 h (median 0.77 h).
- PEPlife2 dose route: IV|PO.
- Curated extraction: study types in vivo; 5 agents, 3 with a body-quoted half-life.
- Curated in vivo routes: iv|not_administered|oral.
- Bioanalytical method: LC-MS/MS for plasma pharmacokinetic samples (Shimadzu HPLC with Applied Biosystems API 4000 QTRAP, positive-mode MRM, Agilent Poroshell 120 EC-C18 column, 13C,15N2-Gem and 13C,15N2-dFdU internal standards); UPLC with photodiode array detection (Waters Acquity H-Class, Acquity HSS T3 column, caffeine internal standard) for portal plasma and intestinal perfusate samples.
- Body-quoted half-lives: V-Gem (5'-l-valyl-gemcitabine): 3.7 min (33%) after intravenous V-Gem; could not be determined after oral dosing [mouse plasma] ;; Gemcitabine (Gem, 2',2'-difluoro-2'-deoxycytidine, dFdC): 42.5 min (44%) after intravenous gemcitabine; 29.8 min (41%) after oral gemcitabine [mouse plasma] ;; dFdU (2',2'-difluoro-2'-deoxyuridine): 106 min (32%) after IV Gem; 210 min (41%) after oral Gem; 128 min (38%) after IV V-Gem; 216 min (9%) after oral V-Gem [mouse plasma].
- Caveats: Cmax and Tmax are reported for every analyte and are captured above; note that for intravenous dosing the table footnote states 'C0 reported for Cmax', so the IV Cmax values (Gem 67.2 uM, V-Gem 32.7 uM) are back-extrapolated initial concentrations rather than observed peaks, and no IV Tmax is given. Oral Tmax values (Gem 30 min, V-Gem 3.5 min, dFdU 45 min) reflect absorption but the paper reports NO absorption half-life and NO absorption rate constant anywhere, so half_life_absorption is 'not reported' throughout and nothing was converted. All T1/2 values are the terminal half-life from non-compartmental analysis in Phoenix WinNonlin 8.2: 'The terminal half-life (T1/2) of Gem following intravenous Gem administration (42.5 min)'. Parameters are geometric mean (geometric CV%) except Tmax (median, min-max) and Foral (dose-normalised AUCinf ratio). CND in Table 3 means could not be determined. Per-analyte values that could not fit the single headline fields are preserved in pk_quote and notes_agent. Procedural and bioanalytical agents not treated as study articles: sodium pentobarbital (40-60 mg/kg intraperitoneal) for perfusion anaesthesia, tetrahydrouridine (THU) added to collection tubes to block ex vivo deamination, EDTA-K3 anticoagulant, and the stable-isotope internal standards 13C,15N2-Gem and 13C,15N2-dFdU. Study_types lists only in vivo: the single-pass jejunal perfusion is performed in situ in an anaesthetised living mouse, and the blank-mouse-plasma calibrator/stability samples are bioanalytical assay validation rather than an ex vivo treatment study. Prior in vitro work with recombinant human CDA, Caco-2 monolayers and simulated gastric fluid is cited in the Discussion as previous studies, not performed here.

Park J (2021) Effect of C-terminus Conjugation via Different Conjugation Chemistries on In Vivo Activity of Albumin-Conjugated Recombinant GLP-1. *Pharmaceutics*.
DOI: https://doi.org/10.3390/pharmaceutics13020263

- Contributes **4 records**, 2 unique sequences; 4 carry a numeric half-life.
- Recovered design: in vivo PK; PEPlife2 `Vivo/Vitro`: in vivo.

- Evidence available: **curated extraction** (PMC PMC7919490).
- Reported half-life: 3.2-9 h (median 5.2 h).
- PEPlife2 dose route: IV.
- Curated extraction: study types in vivo|in vitro; 7 agents, 2 with a body-quoted half-life.
- Curated in vivo routes: iv|not_administered|not_reported|sc.
- Bioanalytical method: Sandwich ELISA (anti-albumin rabbit capture antibody, anti-GLP-1 mouse detection antibody, anti-mouse HRP-conjugated IgG, TMB substrate, A450).
- Body-quoted half-lives: GLP1_8G37C-HSA: 9.0 h [mouse serum/plasma (BALB/c); body uses both 'serum concentrations' and 'plasma half-lives'] ;; GLP1_8A37C-HSA: 7.1 h [mouse serum/plasma (BALB/c)].
- Caveats: TRAP CHECK: the PEPLIFE2 header lists T1/2a values of 3.2 h (GLP1_8G37C-HSA) and 3.3 h (GLP1_8A37C-HSA). No alpha/distribution-phase half-life appears anywhere in the body of this article; the body reports only the terminal plasma half-lives (9.0 h and 7.1 h). The 3.2/3.3 h values are database metadata and were therefore NOT extracted. PK was measured for only two of the four GLP1-HSA conjugates (GLP1_8G37C-HSA and GLP1_8A37C-HSA); GLP1_8G37AzF-HSA was only speculated to be comparable and GLP1_8G16AzF-HSA half-life values are citations of prior work. Group size discrepancy: Methods 2.8 states six IPGTT groups of n = 6/group, whereas the Figure 7 caption states n = 3/group; both are recorded. No Cmax, tmax or concentration-time AUC is reported anywhere in the body; the AUC in Figure 7B is a blood-glucose AUC from the IPGTT, not a PK exposure metric. Amino acid sequences of the GLP-1 variants are referenced in the Figure 2A caption but the one-letter sequences are not printed in the body text, so all sequences are recorded as 'not reported'. Curation rule applied: procedural challenge/probe substances (intraperitoneal glucose, 1.5 g/kg, used for the IPGTT; TCO-Cy5.5 dye used for in-gel labelling) are documented here rather than listed as agents, because they are assay reagents rather than test articles and the role enum has no category for them. Serum samples were assayed by ELISA as bioanalysis of in vivo samples; this was not classified as an ex vivo study type.

- Bioanalytical method: Quantitative sandwich ELISA (goat anti-mouse IgG1-Fc capture, mouse anti-NRG1 detection antibody, goat anti-human Fc-specific HRP conjugate, TMB substrate, A450), with parallel Fc-domain and C-kappa light-chain detection formats; non-compartmental PK analysis in Phoenix 64 WinNonlin 8.1.
- Body-quoted half-lives: NRG1-MFc (wild-type neuregulin-1 EGF domain fused to monomeric Fc): 1.4 h (terminal half-life) [huFcRn transgenic mouse serum] ;; 1F7-MFc (HER4-selective NRG1 variant H2E, K24G, P29H fused to monomeric Fc): 6.6 h (terminal half-life) [huFcRn transgenic mouse serum] ;; ALM6 (parental antibody ligand mimetic; NRG1 EGF domain grafted into b12 IgG1 CDR3H with (G4S)2 linkers on both sides): 1.4 h (terminal half-life) [huFcRn transgenic mouse serum] ;; ALM6-1F7 (HER4-selective 1F7 ligand variant plugged into the ALM6 antibody scaffold): 6.6 h (terminal half-life) [huFcRn transgenic mouse serum].

The body describes these as in vivo PK studies in huFcRn transgenic mice, not a serum stability assay. Route of administration and number of animals per group are never stated anywhere in the body or Methods; only the 5 mg/kg dose from Table 3 is given. The Methods 'In vivo PK assays' section describes only the ELISA and the non-compartmental analysis, with no animal husbandry, ethics statement, strain designation, sex, age, or group size. No amino acid sequences are printed in the body other than the 15-residue fragment NEFTGDRGQNYVMAS; the full NRG1 EGF-domain and Fc sequences listed in the PeplLife2 metadata do not appear in the body. Table 3 also reports AUCINF and clearance: NRG1-MFc 17 h*ug/mL and 288 mL/h/kg; 1F7-MFc 17 and 292; ALM6 26 and 194; ALM6-1F7 91 and 55. Tmax is not reported for any agent.

- Caveats: In vivo PK is limited to serum OCN-vs-time curves after single i.p. injection of recombinant mouse ucOCN in OCN-deficient (Bglap-/-) mice; half-lives (~182 min glycosylated, ~108 min non-glycosylated) were estimated from the declining phase of those curves, no formal non-compartmental PK parameters (AUC, clearance, Vd) are reported. Cmax not reported numerically; tmax inferred only from the statement that maxima occurred at 30 min. Human ucOCN was tested ex vivo in mouse plasma only, never administered to humans or animals, so there is no clinical arm. Plasmin (0-0.3 U/ml), talabostat, PMSF, EDTA, pepstatin A and Dec-RVKR-CMK were used as ex vivo mechanistic treatments rather than administered test articles and are not listed as agents. Full amino-acid sequences appear only in figure graphics (Fig. 1E, Fig. 5A) and are not printed in the body text.

## PMID 28373972

Schmidt R (2017) In vivo Efficacy and Pharmacokinetics of Optimized Apidaecin Analogs. *Front Chem*.
DOI: https://doi.org/10.3389/fchem.2017.00015

- Contributes **4 records**, 1 unique sequences; 4 carry a numeric half-life.
- Recovered design: in vivo PK; PEPlife2 `Vivo/Vitro`: in vivo.
- Live animal dosed in 4 of 4 records.
- Assay matrices: plasma.
- Evidence available: **curated extraction** (PMC PMC5357639).
- Reported half-life: 0.185-0.563 h (median 0.285 h).
- PEPlife2 dose route: IP.
- Curated extraction: study types in vivo|ex vivo; 7 agents, 2 with a body-quoted half-life.
- Curated in vivo routes: ip|iv|other.
- Curated ex vivo matrices: blood|not_reported|plasma|tissue_organ.
- Bioanalytical method: RP-HPLC (Alliance 2695) coupled online to ESI-QqLIT-MS (4000 QTRAP with TurboV ion-spray source) in positive multiple reaction monitoring mode, quantified against isotope-labelled peptide standards (LOQ 19–59 ng/mL); metabolite identification by nanoACQUITY UPLC coupled to ESI-LTQ Orbitrap XL MS; peritoneal lavage stability by RP-HPLC with UV absorbance at 214 nm.
- Body-quoted half-lives: Api88: 17.3 min (i.p. 5 mg/kg) and 11.1 min (i.p. 20 mg/kg) [mouse plasma (female NMRI, intraperitoneal)] ;; Api137: 33.8 min (i.p. 5 mg/kg) and 16.9 min (i.p. 20 mg/kg) [mouse plasma (female NMRI, intraperitoneal)].
- Caveats: All PK parameters were taken from Table 1 and the Results body. Table 1 reports single mean values without error terms, calculated with PKSolver 'using the concentrations for the terminal elimination phase determined by the software'. Half-lives are route- and dose-dependent: i.p. 5 mg/kg gives 17.3 min (Api88) and 33.8 min (Api137); i.p. 20 mg/kg gives 11.1 min and 16.9 min. No half-life was calculated for the intravenous route; after i.v. 5 mg/kg, plasma levels fell to around the LOQ by 10 min (Api88 1 μg/mL and Api137 0.4 μg/mL at 5 min). No Tmax is tabulated; maxima coincided with the earliest sampling times. Group-size discrepancy: Methods state n = 4 per time point per peptide for the i.v. CD-1 study while the Fig. 2 caption states n = 5; Fig. 4 states n = 5 organs while the i.p. Methods state n = 7. The frequently quoted in vitro mouse serum half-lives (~5 min for Api88, ~6 h for Api137, ~3 h for Onc72, >8 h for Onc112) appear in the Introduction and Discussion but are explicitly attributed to earlier publications (Berthold et al.; Czihal et al.; Knappe et al.) and were not measured in this study, so no in vitro study type is recorded. Likewise the MIC values (~4 μg/mL against E. coli ATCC 25922) and ribosome Kd values are cited from prior work. Onc72 and Onc112 were tested here only ex vivo in peritoneal lavage; their in vivo efficacy data are cited from Schmidt et al.

## PMID 29464007

Cui G (2018) Diagnosis of LVAD Thrombus using a High-Avidity Fibrin-Specific 99mTc Probe. *Theranostics*.
DOI: https://doi.org/10.7150/thno.20271

- Contributes **4 records**, 2 unique sequences; 4 carry a numeric half-life.
- Recovered design: in vivo PK; PEPlife2 `Vivo/Vitro`: in vivo.
- Live animal dosed in 4 of 4 records.
- Assay matrices: plasma.
- Evidence available: **curated extraction** (PMC PMC5817118).
- Reported half-life: 0.0833-2.9 h (median 1.11 h).
- PEPlife2 dose route: Injection|N.A..
- Curated extraction: study types in vivo|ex vivo|in vitro; 4 agents, 2 with a body-quoted half-life.
- Curated in vivo routes: iv|not_administered.
- Curated ex vivo matrices: blood|not_reported|plasma|tissue_organ.
- Bioanalytical method: Gamma counting of 99mTc radioactivity in timed, weighed blood and tissue samples with a calibrated well counter (Wizard 3, Perkin-Elmer), decay-corrected and fit to bi-exponential models in MatLab; radio-thin layer chromatography used for radiochemical purity and planar scintigraphy/NanoSPECT-CT for imaging..
- Body-quoted half-lives: 99mTc-F4A (tetrameric PEG-linked fibrin probe): 124.7 ± 41.3 min [mouse blood (percent injected radioactivity per gram blood)] ;; 99mTc-F1A (monomeric bifunctional fibrin probe): 174.2 ± 26.2 min [mouse blood (percent injected radioactivity per gram blood)].
- Caveats: Cmax and tmax are not reported for either probe; dosing was intravenous, so no absorption phase exists and no absorption half-life is reported. Both probes were fit to a two-compartment bi-exponential model: the alpha distribution half-lives (99mTc-F4A 5.0 ± 1.9 min; 99mTc-F1A 8.6 ± 1.9 min) are recorded in the pk_quote fields and were deliberately NOT placed in the headline half_life field, which carries the beta elimination half-life. AUC was not reported. The body calls the slow phase the 'beta elimination half-life', so half_life_phase is given as terminal/beta with half_life_elimination populated from the same value. The PEPLIFE2 header lists the F1A peptide sequence as QWECPYGLCWIQ; the article body prints QIWCLGYPCEWQ and the body was used. Procedural agents administered but not tested as study articles are recorded here rather than as agent objects: ketamine (85 mg/kg)/xylazine (10 mg/kg) plus 1-2% isoflurane for anaesthesia, and Rose Bengal dye (60 ug/g body weight by tail vein, 20 mg/mL) with HeNe laser photoactivation to create the carotid thrombus model. Unlabeled (cold) F1A and excess cysteine were used only as in vitro competitors. Human LVADs came from patients but no patient was dosed, so the study is not classified as clinical.

Shelukhina IV (2018) Azemiopsin, a Selective Peptide Antagonist of Muscle Nicotinic Acetylcholine Receptor: Preclinical Evaluation as a Local Muscle Relaxant. *Toxins (Basel)*.

- Contributes **4 records**, 1 unique sequences; 4 carry a numeric half-life.
- Recovered design: in vivo PK; PEPlife2 `Vivo/Vitro`: in vivo.
- Live animal dosed in 4 of 4 records.
- Assay matrices: blood.
- Evidence available: **curated extraction** (PMC PMC5793121).
- Reported half-life: 0.26-0.68 h (median 0.295 h).
- PEPlife2 dose route: IM|IV.
- Curated extraction: study types in vivo|in vitro|ex vivo; 5 agents, 1 with a body-quoted half-life.
- Curated in vivo routes: im|iv|other|sc.
- Curated ex vivo matrices: blood|serum|tissue_organ.
- Bioanalytical method: Gamma counting of the 125I radiolabel in whole blood (Wallac 1470 WIZARD Gamma Counter); cpm converted to peptide concentration. Az itself was purified/characterised by RP-HPLC and HPLC-MS..
- Body-quoted half-lives: [125I]-Az (di-iodinated azemiopsin): Excretion T1/2: 0.30 h (im 0.25 mg/kg), 0.68 h (im 0.50 mg/kg), 0.26 h (iv 0.25 mg/kg), 0.29 h (iv 0.50 mg/kg); narrative text states 15-20 min iv and 20-40 min im [mouse blood (male ICR mice)].
- Caveats: PK was performed only on the di-iodinated radiolabelled analogue [125I]-Az, not on unlabelled Az; the body explicitly says the di-iodinated product was used because mono-iodination was not achievable, and the Discussion speculates iodination lowers toxicity. The peptide's amino-acid sequence is never printed in the body (only His9, Trp3/Trp4, Pro15 positions are named), so sequence is recorded as not reported despite being present in the PEPLIFE2 metadata header. Blood was sampled at 5, 15, 30, 60 min and 1, 2, 4 and 24 h. Additional tested substances not itemised as agents: trinitrobenzenesulfonic acid and bovine serum albumin (immunisation antigens), Freund's adjuvant, and the mutagenicity positive controls ethylnitrosurea (6.25 and 12.5 μg/mL) and methylcholanthrene (2.5 and 5 μg/mL). Rat subchronic toxicity used 36 male and 36 SD female rats in 3 groups.

- Reported half-life: 25.3-33.6 h (median 28.1 h).
- PEPlife2 dose route: IV|SC.
- Curated extraction: study types in vivo|in vitro; 9 agents, 7 with a body-quoted half-life.
- Curated in vivo routes: iv|not_reported|sc.
- Bioanalytical method: Glucagon-like peptide-1 (active) ELISA kit (Millipore) with fluorescence detection; PK parameters calculated with Phoenix WinNonlin v6.4.
- Body-quoted half-lives: CH70-EDA-(LMDS)-GLP-1C (conjugate 1): T1/2 elim = 32.9 h (s.c., 100 nmol/kg); T1/2 elim = 23.4 h (i.v., 1 mg/kg screening) [mouse plasma (immunoreactive GLP-1)] ;; CH90-EDA-(LMDS)-GLP-1C (conjugate 2): T1/2 elim = 25.3 h (i.v.); 30.3 h (s.c.) [mouse plasma (immunoreactive GLP-1)] ;; HPN50-EDA-(LMDS)-GLP-1C (conjugate 3): T1/2 elim = 33.6 h (i.v.); 25.8 h (s.c.) [mouse plasma (immunoreactive GLP-1)] ;; HPN50-EDA-(BMPS)-GLP-1C (conjugate 4): T1/2 elim = 23.8 h (i.v.) [mouse plasma (immunoreactive GLP-1)] ;; CH10-EDA-(KMUS)-GLP-1C: T1/2 elim = 16 h [mouse plasma (immunoreactive GLP-1)] ;; PEG30k-GLP-1C: T1/2 elim = 17.5 h [mouse plasma (immunoreactive GLP-1)] ;; Liraglutide (Victoza): T1/2 elim = 8.9 h (s.c., 100 nmol/kg) [mouse plasma (immunoreactive GLP-1)].
- Caveats: All half-lives in this paper are explicitly labelled 'T1/2 elim' (elimination half-life, in hours), so the phase comes from the parameter name rather than inference. No absorption half-life and no rate constant (ka/ke) is reported anywhere in the body. Cmax/tmax are reported for conjugate 1 and liraglutide in the text and for conjugates 2 and 3 in Table 1; tmax is listed as 'NA' for the i.v. arms. Screening series not enumerated as individual agents: the body reports that a large panel of CH conjugates varying in CH size (CH10-CH140), amine modifier (EDA/HDA/BAMB/BAMC) and linker (AMAS, BMPS, GMBS, EMCS, HMCS, KMUS, LMDS, NMTS, OMHS) gave 'significantly prolonged half-lives (8.1-38.5 h)', reported only as a range plotted in Figure 4A; individual values are not printed, so only the specifically quantified conjugates are listed as agents. Group sizes: n = 3 for PK, n = 6 for IPGTT/PD. Discussion mentions 'consecutive iv injections of CH in rats at 100 mg/kg for 28 days' and that 'CH circulates in humans' as preliminary/unpublished observations with no data or design given; rat and human are therefore NOT listed in in_vivo_species or as clinical/in vivo study types. Curation rule applied: the intraperitoneal glucose challenge (1 g/kg) used for the IPGTT is a procedural probe and is documented here rather than listed as an agent. Plasma ELISA is bioanalysis of in vivo samples and was not classified as an ex vivo study type.

Group size for the pharmacokinetic study is likewise absent; the only n in the body is n = 6 for the Fig. 2 growth comparison. Amino acid sequences are presented only as an image in Fig. 1, so no one-letter sequence could be taken from the body; the PeplLife2 sequence LQEHPQARKYKGAQKKGLSKGCFGLKLDRIGSMSGLGC does not appear as text in the body. The four PeplLife2 half-life rows map exactly onto the body values: 4A t1/2,alpha ~40 h and t1/2,beta ~212 h; 4B t1/2,alpha ~60 h and t1/2,beta ~610 h, all in adult CD1 mouse plasma. The reported Cmax and Cmin values for 4A and 4B are SIMULATED/predicted from the fitted PK, not directly observed; this is stated explicitly ('Assuming dose linearity, simulations for conjugate 4A indicated...'). Cynomolgus monkey pharmacokinetics are referred to in the body but the data appear only in SI Appendix Figs. S5 and S6. Serum ELISA is bioanalysis of in vivo samples and was not classified as an ex vivo study type.

## PMID 36982773

Järveläinen HA (2023) Assessment of the Pharmacokinetics, Disposition, and Duration of Action of the Tumour-Targeting Peptide CEND-1. *Int J Mol Sci*.
DOI: https://doi.org/10.3390/ijms24065700

- Contributes **3 records**, 1 unique sequences; 3 carry a numeric half-life.
- Recovered design: in vivo PK; PEPlife2 `Vivo/Vitro`: in vivo.
- Live animal dosed in 3 of 3 records.
- Assay matrices: plasma.
- Evidence available: **curated extraction** (PMC PMC10053770).
- Reported half-life: 0.306-0.547 h (median 0.344 h).
- PEPlife2 dose route: IV.
- Curated extraction: study types clinical|in vivo; 7 agents, 2 with a body-quoted half-life.
- Curated in vivo routes: infusion_pump|iv.
- Bioanalytical method: Validated LC-MS/MS for plasma CEND-1 (AB Sciex Triple Quad API 5500, positive ESI, transition m/z 495.4 to 69.9; tolbutamide or leuprorelin acetate internal standards). Tissue disposition measured by quantitative whole-body autoradiography (QWBA) and quantitative radioactivity analysis (QRA) of [3H]-CEND-1. Non-compartmental analysis in Phoenix WinNonlin v6.1..
- Body-quoted half-lives: CEND-1 (iRGD) - preclinical peptide: 0.306 h, 0.344 h and 0.547 h at 1.5, 4.5 and 13.5 mg/kg respectively (mouse, Table 1); text states 'approximately 20-25 min in mice' [mouse plasma] ;; CEND-1 drug product (clinical formulation): 1.956 ± 0.289 h (run-in), 1.725 ± 0.230 h (cycle 1 day 1), 1.598 h (cycle 6 day 1, N = 1) at 3.2 mg/kg; text: 'median half-life values of CEND-1 (T1/2) were between 1.6 and 1.8 h' [human plasma].
- Caveats: UNITS TRAP RESOLVED: Table 1's parameter column is printed as 't1/2h'. It is a half-life expressed in hours, not a rate constant in h-1. Evidence used: the parameter name is t1/2, and the body text states 'The average half-lives were approximately 20-25 min in mice, 30 min in rats and 40 min in dogs' and 'In monkeys, the average half-life was approximately 55 min', which match the tabulated 0.306-0.547 h (mouse), 0.248-0.805 h (rat), 0.615-0.668 h (dog) and 0.888-0.956 h (monkey). No ln(2)/k conversion was performed by the curator. RATE CONSTANT HANDLED SEPARATELY: human Table 2 lists lambda_last of 0.3607, 0.4082 and 0.4338 1/h; these were recorded verbatim in pk_quote and were NOT converted into half-lives, because the same table already reports the fitted t1/2 values. Cmax vs C0: Table 1 reports C0 (concentration back-extrapolated to time zero) for the animal species, not an observed Cmax; the Results text nonetheless describes 'systemic exposure of CEND-1, expressed as Cmax and AUClast' and the Discussion says 'this dose results in a Cmax (C0) of approximately 40 microg/mL'. Both the label and the value are recorded so the distinction is not lost. No tmax is reported for any animal species (intravenous dosing); tmax IS reported for humans (0.063-0.117 h). No absorption half-life is reported for any species, as all dosing was intravenous. DOSE INCONSISTENCY INSIDE THE BODY: Methods 4.1 states '1, 5 and 50 mg/kg for rats, dogs and monkeys', but Table 1 lists 1, 5 and 75 mg/kg for rats and dogs; both are recorded. Study-type scope: this article covers mouse, rat, dog, cynomolgus monkey and human; the mouse PK values (t1/2 0.306/0.344/0.547 h) are the ones matching the PEPLIFE2 header rows. Curation rule applied: procedural probe, contrast and anaesthetic substances are documented here rather than listed as agents - Evans blue (33.3 mg/kg i.v., extracted from tissue in N,N-dimethylformamide and quantified at 600 nm), Gd-DTPA, Gd-DOTA and gadoxetic acid (Gd-EOB-DTPA) MRI contrast agents, and ketamine (70 mg/kg) plus xylazine (10 mg/kg) and isoflurane as anaesthetics. nab-paclitaxel and gemcitabine are co-administered background chemotherapy rather than true comparator arms; they are given role 'comparator' only because the schema role enum has no 'co-administered therapy' option. Excision and extraction of tissues for QRA and Evans blue quantification were treated as bioanalysis of in vivo samples, not as a separate ex vivo experiment, consistent with the handling of plasma assays.

- Reported half-life: 1.8-7 h (median 3.9 h).
- PEPlife2 dose route: SC.
- Curated extraction: study types in vivo|in vitro; 6 agents, 3 with a body-quoted half-life.
- Curated in vivo routes: ip|sc.
- Bioanalytical method: LC-MS/MS of plasma; PK parameters calculated with BAPP 2.2 (China Pharmaceutical University).
- Body-quoted half-lives: Lip-Di-GLP-1 (lipidated dimerized GLP-1): 7.0 ± 0.7 h [Kunming mouse plasma] ;; Di-GLP-1 (dimerized GLP-1): 1.8 ± 0.2 h [Kunming mouse plasma] ;; Liraglutide (chemically synthesized): 3.9 ± 0.3 h [Kunming mouse plasma].
- Caveats: The body reports t1/2 and AUCinf only; NO Cmax and NO tmax appear anywhere in the body, because the full PK parameter set is deferred to Table S3 in the Electronic Supplementary Information. AUCinf values (7456.6 ± 564.4; 2693.6 ± 175.5; 1637.3 ± 39.3) are printed without units in the body and are recorded verbatim on that basis. The half-life is labelled only 't1/2' with no phase, so half_life_phase is 'unspecified' and both half_life_absorption and half_life_elimination are left 'not reported' - the paper does not fit or report a two-phase model. Group-size discrepancy: Methods state n = 18/21/24 total animals (destructive sampling, mice sacrificed at each of 8 timepoints), while the Figure 1F caption states n = 3, i.e. 3 mice per timepoint; both are recorded. Sequences: the body describes the construct in words (Gly8-Cys31-GLP-1 plus bis-maleimide amine plus palmitic acid) but never prints a one-letter sequence, so all sequences are 'not reported'; the sequences in the PEPLIFE2 header are database metadata and were not used. Curation rule applied: procedural probe and challenge substances are documented here rather than listed as agents - paracetamol (100 mg/kg orally, plasma levels measured by LC-MS/MS as a gastric-emptying probe) and the intraperitoneal glucose challenge (2 g/kg in Kunming mice, 1 g/kg in db/db mice). The published year field in the file header (2019) conflicts with the PMID vintage; not an extraction field.

No two-phase model, no absorption half-life and no rate constant appear in the body. No Cmax, tmax or AUC is reported because the assay produced relative (densitometric) serum concentrations rather than absolute values. The elimination value is taken as the headline half_life and half_life_elimination; the distribution value is preserved in pk_quote because the schema has no distribution field. For WT-Rb the body reports an 'initial half-life' of 20 min and explicitly states that the terminal half-life could not be calculated, so the 20 min value is classified as the initial (distribution/alpha) phase and half_life_elimination is left 'not reported'. Dosing was intravenous, so no absorption phase exists or is reported. AUC is given only for Pal-Rb ('about 747%·h'); WT-Rb AUC is described only as 4.2-fold lower and is therefore recorded as not reported. No error terms are given for any PK parameter. Cmax and tmax are not reported. Serum concentrations are relative (3 min = 100%), not absolute. The PEPLIFE2 header rows list repebody amino-acid sequences, but those are database metadata and the body prints no sequence, so sequence is 'not reported'. The mouse serum albumin binding ELISA uses purified MSA in buffer on a coated plate and is classified as in vitro, not ex vivo.

## PMID 31100806

Grishin AV (2019) The Influence of Dimerization on the Pharmacokinetics and Activity of an Antibacterial Enzyme Lysostaphin. *Molecules*.
DOI: https://doi.org/10.3390/molecules24101879

- Contributes **3 records**, 3 unique sequences; 3 carry a numeric half-life.
- Recovered design: in vivo PK; PEPlife2 `Vivo/Vitro`: in vivo.
- Live animal dosed in 3 of 3 records.
- Assay matrices: plasma|serum.
- Evidence available: **curated extraction** (PMC PMC6572387).
- Reported half-life: 0.342-1 h (median 0.375 h).
- PEPlife2 dose route: IV.
- Curated extraction: study types in vivo|in vitro; 4 agents, 2 with a body-quoted half-life.
- Curated in vivo routes: im|iv|not_administered|sc.
- Bioanalytical method: Sandwich ELISA: rabbit anti-lysostaphin antiserum capture (1/500), murine 2F9 monoclonal antibody detection (1/300), HRP-conjugated anti-mouse secondary, TMB substrate, absorbance at 450 nm; terminal half-life and AUC by non-compartmental analysis.
- Body-quoted half-lives: Lst-HDD (lysostaphin fused to an antiparallel α-helical homodimerization domain via a glycine-serine spacer): 3.1 ± 0.6 h (terminal half-life) [rat plasma] ;; Lysostaphin (recombinant, monomeric): 1.5 ± 0.7 h (terminal half-life) [rat plasma].
- Caveats: IMPORTANT SPECIES CAVEAT: all pharmacokinetic work in this paper was done in RATS (female Wistar, n = 4 per group), not mice. The body does state mouse half-life values for lysostaphin, Cpl-1 and LytA, but only in the Introduction as citations of prior literature, not as data generated here: 'The estimated serum half-life of anti-staphylococcal lysin lysostaphin in mice was less than 1 h, and the half-life times of anti-pneumococcal lysins Cpl-1 and LytA were measured to be 20.5 and 22.5 min, respectively.' Cpl-1 and LytA were never produced, administered or assayed in this study and are therefore not listed as agents. Reported PK: lysostaphin terminal t1/2 1.5 ± 0.7 h and AUC 3.5 ± 2.4 mg·h/L; Lst-HDD terminal t1/2 3.1 ± 0.6 h and AUC 9.1 ± 2.8 mg·h/L. No Cmax or Tmax was determined; the first sample was at 25 min, where concentrations were 2.8 ± 0.2 µg/mL (lysostaphin) and 1.6 ± 0.1 µg/mL (Lst-HDD) against a theoretical post-injection concentration of ~50 µg/mL, indicating ~95% had already been eliminated. Amino-acid sequences appear only as a figure graphic (Fig. 1B) and are not extractable from the body text.

Handgraaf HJM (2017) Real-time near-infrared fluorescence imaging using cRGD-ZW800-1 for intraoperative visualization of multiple cancer types. *Oncotarget*.
DOI: https://doi.org/10.18632/oncotarget.15486

- Contributes **3 records**, 1 unique sequences; 3 carry a numeric half-life.
- Recovered design: in vivo PK; PEPlife2 `Vivo/Vitro`: in vivo.
- Live animal dosed in 3 of 3 records.
- Assay matrices: serum.
- Evidence available: **curated extraction** (PMC PMC5400565).
- Reported half-life: 0.243-1.19 h (median 0.422 h).
- PEPlife2 dose route: IV.
- Curated extraction: study types in vivo|ex vivo|in vitro; 2 agents, 1 with a body-quoted half-life.
- Curated in vivo routes: iv|other.
- Curated ex vivo matrices: blood|not_reported|serum|tissue_organ.
- Bioanalytical method: NIR fluorescence measurement of whole blood in 60 microL capillary tubes using the FLARE imaging system at multiple exposure times, back-calculated against a mouse-serum calibration curve; PK analysis in NONMEM.
- Body-quoted half-lives: cRGD-ZW800-1: 71.1 ± 9.4 min (mean terminal half-life) [mouse serum (peripheral blood, tumour-free mice)].
- Caveats: Cmax/tmax ARE reported here: maximal serum concentration 1.17 microM measured at 1 minute post injection (the first post-dose sampling point of a -5/1/6/10/20/30/40/50/60/90/120/240 min schedule), so tmax is the earliest sample rather than a fitted peak. No absorption half-life exists for this study because dosing was intravenous. CONFLICTING HALF-LIVES IN THE BODY: the Results give a mean terminal half-life of 71.1 ± 9.4 min, while the Discussion refers to a 'mean blood half-life of 25 min' with no phase label. Both are in the body; the named terminal value was taken as the headline. The 25.3 ± 6.4 min and 14.6 ± 1.7 min (distribution) values in the PEPLIFE2 header are database metadata; the distribution half-life is referenced in the body only as 'see Supplementary Table 2', so it is recorded as not reported. AUC is mentioned in the Figure 6 caption but no numeric value appears in the body. Sequence: the body calls cRGD-ZW800-1 a 'cyclic pentapeptide' and never prints the amino acid sequence; the 'RGDyK' string comes only from the PEPLIFE2 metadata block and was not used. Substances mentioned but excluded from agents, with reasons: ZW800-1 dye alone (evaluated at 24.5 mg/kg but explicitly 'unpublished data'); a rat toxicology study of cRGD-ZW800-1 at 5.0 mg/kg i.v., also explicitly 'unpublished data' - rat is therefore not listed in in_vivo_species; isoflurane (anaesthetic), propidium iodide, ToPro3 and anti-integrin/isotype antibodies (assay reagents). Ex vivo classification is based on the mouse-serum calibration curve (compound added to serum outside the body) and on ex vivo imaging of excised organs and tumours.

## PMID 19602537

Pocai A (2009) Glucagon-like peptide 1/glucagon receptor dual agonism reverses obesity in mice. *Diabetes*.
DOI: https://doi.org/10.2337/db09-0278

- Contributes **3 records**, 2 unique sequences; 2 carry a numeric half-life.
- Recovered design: in vivo PK; PEPlife2 `Vivo/Vitro`: in vivo.
- Live animal dosed in 3 of 3 records.
- Assay matrices: plasma.
- Evidence available: **curated extraction** (PMC PMC2750209).
- Reported half-life: 1.7-10 h (median 5.85 h).
- PEPlife2 dose route: N.A..
- Curated extraction: study types in vivo|ex vivo|in vitro; 5 agents, 2 with a body-quoted half-life.
- Curated in vivo routes: ip|not_reported|sc.
- Curated ex vivo matrices: not_reported|plasma|tissue_organ.
- Bioanalytical method: In vitro cell-based cAMP bioassay of GLP1R agonist potency (CHO cells stably transfected with human GLP1R), quantified against a standard curve of peptide spiked into mouse plasma.
- Body-quoted half-lives: DualAG: 1.7 h [mouse plasma (circulating bioactive peptide, lean C57Bl/6 mice)] ;; oxyntomodulin (OXM), native: ~8–12 min [not reported (stated only as in vivo T1/2 for native OXM)].
- Caveats: PK values are reported only for DualAG, and only in a single RESULTS sentence: Tmax 5 h and T1/2 1.7 h after 3 mg/kg s.c. in lean C57Bl/6 mice. No error terms, no group size, no AUC and no numeric Cmax are given for the PK study; 'peak plasma peptide concentrations (Cmax) were achieved ∼5 h after subcutaneous injection' reports a time, not a concentration, so cmax is recorded as not reported. GLPAG PK is described only qualitatively as comparable to DualAG. The paper reports a single half-life without naming a phase, so half_life_phase is 'unspecified' and both half_life_absorption and half_life_elimination are left 'not reported' per protocol; do not read 1.7 h as a fitted elimination-phase value. DISCREPANCY with the PEPLIFE2 header rows: the header lists Oxm at '~8-12 hours', but the body twice states minutes ('T1/2 ∼12 min' in INTRO, '∼8–12 min for native OXM' in RESULTS); the body value in minutes is recorded here. The header also lists the assay as RP-HPLC and ESI-MS; in the body RP-HPLC and electrospray MS were used for peptide synthesis, purification and characterisation, while plasma peptide concentrations were measured by a cell-based cAMP bioassay. Sequences were not extracted because the PEPLIFE2 header is database metadata and the body does not print the amino-acid sequences. Roles: dextrose is a metabolic challenge agent rather than a test article; the protocol role enum does not cover this, so 'other' is used. Anaesthetics (isofluorane) are procedural and are not listed as agents.

## PMID 38863646

He C (2024) Construction and Application of a PD-L1-Targeted Multimodal Diagnostic and Dual-Functional Theranostics Nanoprobe. *Int J Nanomedicine*.
DOI: https://doi.org/10.2147/IJN.S461701

- Contributes **2 records**, 0 unique sequences; 2 carry a numeric half-life.
- Recovered design: in vivo PK; PEPlife2 `Vivo/Vitro`: in vivo.
- Live animal dosed in 2 of 2 records.
- Assay matrices: blood.
- Evidence available: **curated extraction** (PMC PMC11166151).
- Reported half-life: 0.123-1.55 h (median 0.839 h).
- PEPlife2 dose route: Injection.
- Curated extraction: study types in vivo|in vitro; 9 agents, 1 with a body-quoted half-life.
- Curated in vivo routes: iv|not_administered.
- Bioanalytical method: Radioactivity measurement of weighed blood samples with a gamma-counter (89Zr); pharmacokinetic parameters fitted in GraphPad Prism 9.0.
- Body-quoted half-lives: (89Zr, Mn)-WPMNs (89Zr-radiolabelled, Mn2+-chelated, WL12-SH- and PEG-modified melanin nanoparticles): metabolic half-life 0.1234 h and distribution half-life 1.554 h [KM mouse blood (samples collected from the orbital vein)].
- Caveats: PK is reported for (89Zr, Mn)-WPMNs only. The body reports two half-lives with non-standard phase names: 'metabolic half-life' 0.1234 h and 'distribution half-life' 1.554 h. Note the naming is inverted relative to the usual convention - here the faster value is called metabolic and the slower value distribution - and the paper never uses the words 'elimination' or 'terminal'. Both values are therefore recorded verbatim in half_life with half_life_phase 'unspecified', and half_life_absorption and half_life_elimination are left 'not reported' rather than mapping the slow phase onto elimination. Dosing was intravenous, so no absorption phase exists. Both are stated as half-lives in hours, not rate constants. No error terms, Cmax, tmax or AUC are reported for the blood PK; the reported concentrations elsewhere are biodistribution %ID/g at fixed timepoints (liver 39.43 ± 10.03 %ID/g, spleen 25.19 ± 0.43 %ID/g at 24 h). Group size for the PK cohort is not stated. Two different 89Zr physical half-lives appear in the body for context ('89Zr (1/2t=78.4 h)' in the INTRO); the abstract's 100.8 h value was not used. The WL12-SH amino-acid sequence is not printed in the body and the PEPLIFE2 header sequence field is blank, so sequence is 'not reported'. Anaesthetics (isoflurane, 4% chloral hydrate) and the 808 nm laser irradiation are procedural interventions and are not listed as agents.

Moore BD (2023) A C1qTNF3 collagen domain fusion chaperones diverse secreted proteins and anti-Aβ scFvs: Applications for gene therapies. *Mol Ther Methods Clin Dev*.
DOI: https://doi.org/10.1016/j.omtm.2023.101146

- Contributes **2 records**, 2 unique sequences; 2 carry a numeric half-life.
- Recovered design: in vivo PK; PEPlife2 `Vivo/Vitro`: in vivo.
- Live animal dosed in 2 of 2 records.
- Assay matrices: plasma.
- Evidence available: **curated extraction** (PMC PMC10679951).
- Reported half-life: 0.74-1.82 h (median 1.28 h).
- PEPlife2 dose route: IP.
- Curated extraction: study types in vivo|ex vivo|in vitro; 20 agents, 2 with a body-quoted half-life.
- Curated in vivo routes: icv|ip|not_administered|other|sc.
- Curated ex vivo matrices: not_reported|plasma|tissue_organ.
- Bioanalytical method: Direct ELISA with Abeta40 (5 μg/well) as capture and NeutrAvidin-HRP as detection, against a purified scFv standard.
- Body-quoted half-lives: biotinylated scFv9-CD (anti-Abeta scFv9 fused to the 126-aa C1qTNF3 collagen domain): 1.82 h [mouse plasma (female B6C3F1)] ;; biotinylated scFv9 (anti-Abeta single-chain variable fragment): 0.74 h [mouse plasma (female B6C3F1)].
- Caveats: PK was measured only for biotinylated scFv9 and scFv9-CD after a single 250 μg IP dose; no PK was measured for scFvB11, scFvB11-CD, scFv12, scFv12-CD or CD alone. Half-life was 'calculated by nonlinear regression analysis of one-phase decay', i.e. a single-phase fit, and the paper never names the phase, so half_life_phase is 'unspecified' and both half_life_absorption and half_life_elimination are left 'not reported' per protocol - the 0.74 h and 1.82 h values must not be read as fitted elimination-phase parameters. No error terms, Cmax, tmax or AUC are reported. Note that the route is intraperitoneal, so an absorption phase certainly exists physiologically but was not modelled or reported. Internal inconsistency in the body: Figure 1D/1E label scFv12 as 'against N-terminal a-syn', while Figures 4-7 and the accompanying Results describe scFv12 as an anti-Abeta scFv derived from mAb 2B12; both usages are recorded as-is. Figure 6B legend writes 'scFvB12' where the Results text uses 'scFv12'. The Figure 3H caption states 100 mM Abeta42 whereas the Methods state 100 μM. The rAAV titre in the Methods is printed as '(10e genomes per milliliter)', which appears garbled in the source text, so no titre is recorded. The PEPLIFE2 header lists full scFv9 and scFv9-CD sequences, but that is database metadata and the body prints no sequences, so sequence is 'not reported' except for the ISEVKMDAEFRH immunogen fragment which is printed in the Figure 4A caption. Role 'other' is used for the immunogen and for synthetic Abeta42 because the protocol role enum does not cover immunogens or assay substrates.

Additional in vitro tool compounds used but not itemised as agents: cycloheximide (2.5 mg/mL), bafilomycin A1 (10 nM), bortezomib (20 nM), PKH26 dye and HA-ubiquitin transfection. - Caveats: No numeric elimination half-life (t1/2) is reported for BI-3434 in the body; the Results give MRT, Cmax and tmax and the Discussion refers loosely to a half-life 'up to 6 h'. Stapled secretin has only an upper-bound half-life (<15 min) after i.v. dosing and no other PK parameters. Peptide sequences appear only in the Figure 1 structure image, which is not transcribed in the text, so all sequences are recorded as not reported. The freshly isolated mouse adipocyte lipolysis assay is called 'in vitro' by the authors but was classified ex vivo per protocol; the cell-line cAMP assay is the unambiguous in vitro component. Several body paragraphs in the source file were truncated at 2000 characters.

## PMID 37384895

Ngema LM (2023) Surface Immobilization of Anti-VEGF Peptide on SPIONs for Antiangiogenic and Targeted Delivery of Paclitaxel in Non-Small-Cell Lung Carcinoma. *ACS Appl Bio Mater*.
DOI: https://doi.org/10.1021/acsabm.3c00224

- Contributes **2 records** (1 distinct, the rest are exact repeats), 1 unique sequences; 2 carry a numeric half-life.
- Recovered design: in vivo PK; PEPlife2 `Vivo/Vitro`: in vivo.
- Live animal dosed in 2 of 2 records.
- Assay matrices: plasma.
- Evidence available: **curated extraction** (PMC PMC10354746).
- Reported half-life: 17.1-17.1 h (median 17.1 h).
- PEPlife2 dose route: SC.
- Curated extraction: study types in vivo|in vitro; 8 agents, 2 with a body-quoted half-life.
- Curated in vivo routes: not_administered|not_reported|sc.
- Bioanalytical method: High performance liquid chromatography with UV detection at 227 nm (Flexar LC UV/VIS, PerkinElmer), after liquid-liquid extraction of plasma with 1.25% v/v ethanol in diethyl ether and docetaxel as internal standard; Waters C18 column (250 x 3.9 mm, 5 μm), 20 μL injection, 1.9 mL/min.
- Body-quoted half-lives: CLA-coated PTX-SPIONs@HRH: 17.1 h [mouse plasma (PTX measured; heparin-flushed tubes, cardiac puncture)] ;; Taxol (commercial paclitaxel formulation): 9.0 h [mouse plasma].
- Caveats: UNIT-HEADER CHECK: the PK table has two separate columns, 'T1/2 (h)' and 'Kel (h-1)'. The half-life values recorded here (17.1 h and 9.0 h) come from the column named T1/2 (h) and are confirmed in the Results text as half-lives ('The half-life (T1/2) of commercial PTX was estimated to be 9.0 h'). The elimination rate constants Kel = 0.03 h-1 (nanoformulation) and Kel = 0.07 h-1 (Taxol) are recorded verbatim here and were NOT converted to half-lives. The paper names no kinetic phase for T1/2, so half_life_phase is 'unspecified' and half_life_elimination is left 'not reported', even though the adjacent Kel column is labelled 'elimination rate constant' in the table footnote. Dosing was subcutaneous, so an absorption phase exists and the body states 'PTX from CLA-coated PTX-SPIONs@HRH is slowly absorbed and released into plasma following SC injection', but no absorption half-life was fitted or reported. The PK measurand is paclitaxel, not the HRH peptide. Plasma sampling was destructive (mice euthanized at each of t = 0.5, 1, 4, 8, 24 h) with n = 3 per group. The antitumor study used a 10-fold lower PTX dose for Taxol (1.2 mg/kg) than the nanoformulation dose (12 mg/kg), matched to the 10% drug loading capacity; the PK study states 'equivalent PTX dosage' without giving a number.

No Cmax or tmax values are reported numerically anywhere in the body; peaks are described only as percentage ratios of intact to total GIP (e.g. intact reached 41% of total after IV). AUC is reported only as incremental AUC fold-changes (IV 5.4-fold, SC 8.1-fold, IP 7.8-fold increase with Val-Pyr) and as intact-iAUC expressed as a percentage of total-iAUC (IV placebo 13 ± 1% vs Val-Pyr 74 ± 3%); no absolute AUC with units is given, so auc is 'not reported'. Other PK parameters reported in the body: clearance 207 ± 16 μL/min/g (placebo) vs 45 ± 4 μL/min/g (Val-Pyr); apparent volume of distribution 0.34 ± 0.1 mL/g vs 0.46 ± 0.03 mL/g. The Methods list blood sampling at t = 0, 1, 3, 5, 10 and 20 min but the Results refer to timepoints up to t = 30 min; both recorded as printed. Sequence of GIP(1-42) is not printed in the body.

## PMID 31996466

Wolska A (2020) A dual apolipoprotein C-II mimetic-apolipoprotein C-III antagonist peptide lowers plasma triglycerides. *Sci Transl Med*.
DOI: https://doi.org/10.1126/scitranslmed.aaw7905

- Contributes **2 records**, 1 unique sequences; 2 carry a numeric half-life.
- Recovered design: in vivo PK; PEPlife2 `Vivo/Vitro`: in vivo.
- Live animal dosed in 2 of 2 records.
- Assay matrices: plasma.
- Evidence available: **curated extraction** (PMC PMC8359806).
- Reported half-life: 3-9 h (median 6 h).
- PEPlife2 dose route: IV.
- Curated extraction: study types in vivo|ex vivo|in vitro; 17 agents, 2 with a body-quoted half-life.
- Curated in vivo routes: ip|iv|not_administered|sc.
- Curated ex vivo matrices: not_reported|plasma.
- Bioanalytical method: Liquid chromatography with tandem mass spectrometry (LC-MS/MS) after protein precipitation of EDTA-plasma with 3:1 acetonitrile:methanol, using Delta6PL as internal standard; lower limit of quantitation 0.2 μg/mL, calibration linear from 0.2 to 40 μg/mL. PK parameters fitted by a multi-compartmental model in WinNonLin..
- Body-quoted half-lives: D6PV: 9 h [mouse plasma (EDTA)] ;; D6PV (nonhuman primate arm): 42 to 50 h [Cynomolgus monkey plasma].
- Caveats: Two half-lives are reported for D6PV in mice after IV dosing: a 3 h initial-phase and a 9 h terminal-phase value. Both are described by the authors as elimination half-lives ('the elimination half-life for the initial phase (t1/2,α)' and 'the elimination half-life for the terminal phase (t1/2,z)'), so neither is an absorption half-life; the headline half_life is the terminal 9 h value and both are recorded in half_life_elimination. The 42-50 h terminal half-life is a NONHUMAN PRIMATE value, not a mouse value, and is kept in a separate agent object. Bioavailability after SC dosing was over 65% at 1 and 10 mg/kg in mice and approximately 80% in nonhuman primates; no absorption half-life was fitted. AUC is described only qualitatively ('A nonlinear increase in the area-under-the-curve (AUC) was observed between the 10 and 100 mg/kg dose groups'), with no numeric values or units, so auc is 'not reported'. Other reported mouse PK parameters: steady-state volume of distribution Vss 81 mL/kg and clearance ~8 mL/kg/h. The allometrically scaled human terminal half-life of 3-4 days is a projection, not a measurement, and is deliberately not recorded as a half-life. Methods state the mouse PK study had '4 groups, 3 doses ... and 2 dose routes ... with 18 animals in each group' for 72 mice, which is internally inconsistent (3 x 2 = 6 arms); recorded verbatim.

Seo H (2022) PEGylation improves the therapeutic potential of dimerized translationally controlled tumor protein blocking peptide in ovalbumin-induced mouse model of airway inflammation. *Drug Deliv*.

- Contributes **2 records**, 1 unique sequences; 2 carry a numeric half-life.

- Assay matrices: plasma.
- Evidence available: **curated extraction** (PMC PMC9302014).

- PEPlife2 dose route: IV.
- Curated extraction: study types in vivo|in vitro; 7 agents, 2 with a body-quoted half-life.
- Curated in vivo routes: ip|iv|not_administered.

- Body-quoted half-lives: PEG-dTBP2 (N-terminal mono-PEGylated dTBP2, 10 kDa mPEG): 2.57 ± 1.11 (Table 1 parameter 'T1/2'; the unit is not printed in that table row) [mouse plasma (blood from the orbital sinus of male ICR mice)] ;; dTBP2 (dTCTP-binding peptide 2): 1.01 ± 0.25 (Table 1 parameter 'T1/2'; the unit is not printed in that table row) [mouse plasma (blood from the orbital sinus of male ICR mice)].
- Caveats: PK parameters come from Table 1 after a single 10 mg/kg intravenous dose in male ICR mice, n = 3 per group, calculated by non-compartmental analysis in Phoenix WinNonlin 6.4. UNIT CAVEAT: in the rendered Table 1 the T1/2 row carries no unit, whereas AUC (μg·hr/ml), CL (L/kg/hr) and Vss (L/kg) rows do; the value has been recorded exactly as printed with the missing unit flagged rather than assuming hours. The parameter is unambiguously a half-life, not a rate constant - the table footnote states 'T1/2 terminal half-life' and the Results text says 'Terminal T1/2 and AUC of PEG-dTBP2 increased by about 2.54-fold and 116-fold more than dTBP2' (2.57/1.01 = 2.54, consistent). half_life_phase is therefore 'terminal/beta'. Because the analysis was non-compartmental with a single terminal T1/2 and no two-phase fit, half_life_absorption and half_life_elimination are both left 'not reported'. Dosing was intravenous, so no absorption phase exists. Cmax and tmax are not reported. Clearance and volume of distribution are also tabulated (dTBP2 CL 409.01 ± 98.89 and Vss 487.21 ± 168.28; PEG-dTBP2 CL 3.06 ± 0.57 and Vss 6.35 ± 2.57). Separate in vitro microsomal stability values are reported (30% of dTBP2 versus 75% of PEG-dTBP2 remaining after 30 min with human liver microsomes; 2.5- and 5-fold better stability in human and mouse microsomes respectively) but these are not half-lives and are not placed in any half_life field. The body contains an apparent typographical slip in the IL-8 results - 'the same concentrations of native PEG-dTBP2 reduced the level of IL-8 to 30% and 40%' appears to mean native dTBP2. Role 'other' is used for OVA/alum and recombinant dTCTP because they are disease-model induction and cell-stimulation agents rather than test articles; the protocol role enum does not cover these.

The half-lives quoted in RESULTS 3.2 (117 h human serum, 30 h diabetic rat serum, 60 h in vivo) differ slightly from the abstract's fold-change figures; only the body values were used. Several body paragraphs in the source file were truncated at 2000 characters.

| 5870 | HDL-SP  | 60 Hours | Mice serum  | In Vivo | in vivo PK |
