- Reported half-life: 5.16-36.3 h (median 20.7 h).
- PEPlife2 dose route: SC.
- Curated extraction: study types in vivo|in vitro; 9 agents, 2 with a body-quoted half-life.
- Curated in vivo routes: iv|sc.
- Bioanalytical method: Commercial Exendin-4 enzyme immunoassay (EIA) kit (Phoenix Biotech, USA) for plasma concentrations; PET imaging (Inveon small-animal scanner) and gamma counting (Packard) for radiolabeled analogs.
- Body-quoted half-lives: Abextide (tEB-Exendin-4; truncated Evans blue conjugated to Cys40-Exendin-4): 36.28 ± 7.01 h [plasma (blood samples after subcutaneous administration; Results describe db/db mice, Table 1 caption says SD rats - see notes)] ;; Exendin-4 (native, unmodified): 5.16 ± 5.23 h [plasma (blood samples after subcutaneous administration; Results describe db/db mice, Table 1 caption says SD rats - see notes)].
- Caveats: Discrepancy within the article: the Methods and Results place the pharmacokinetic study in db/db mice ('in vivo pharmacokinetics test of Abextide in db/db mice'), but the Table 1 caption reads 'after subcutaneous administration to SD rats'. The half-life, Cmax, Tmax and AUCinf values in Table 1 are therefore of uncertain species attribution; they are recorded here with the mouse attribution given in the Results text and this conflict flagged. Group size also conflicts: Methods state n = 3/group while the Figure 4A caption states n = 4/group. No amino acid sequence or SMILES is printed in the body for Exendin-4 or Abextide. Blood sampling times for the PK arm are given only as 'predetermined times'; the IPGTT time points were 0, 1, 2, 4, 6, 8, 12, 24, 48, 72, 96 h.

## PMID 28356733

Zhao SJ (2017) A novel selective VPAC2 agonist peptide-conjugated chitosan modified selenium nanoparticles with enhanced anti-type 2 diabetes synergy effects. *Int J Nanomedicine*.
DOI: https://doi.org/10.2147/IJN.S130566

- Contributes **2 records**, 1 unique sequences; 2 carry a numeric half-life.
- Recovered design: in vivo PK; PEPlife2 `Vivo/Vitro`: in vivo.
- Live animal dosed in 2 of 2 records.
- Assay matrices: blood.
- Evidence available: **curated extraction** (PMC PMC5367579).
- Reported half-life: 1.98-14.1 h (median 8.05 h).
- PEPlife2 dose route: IV.
- Curated extraction: study types in vivo|in vitro; 11 agents, 3 with a body-quoted half-life.
- Curated in vivo routes: ip|iv|other.
- Bioanalytical method: LC/MS/MS for in vivo blood concentrations; RP-HPLC (280 nm) for in vitro release, entrapment efficiency and drug loading; ELISA for insulin.
- Body-quoted half-lives: SCD (SeNPs-CTS-DBAYL; DBAYL-conjugated, chitosan-modified selenium nanoparticles): 14.12 h [db/db mouse blood (orbital blood collected 0.5–24 h post dosing)] ;; DBAYL (recombinant PACAP-derived VPAC2 agonist peptide): 1.98 h [mouse (db/db mouse blood; Fig. 2B reports half-life of DBAYL in db/db mice)] ;; BAY55-9837: ~5 min (stated in the Introduction as a previously reported value, not as a measurement made in this study) [in vivo; Fig. 2B states the half-life of BAY55-9837 was determined in db/db mice but no numeric value is given in the body].
- Caveats: Half-life was calculated with a one-compartment elimination model in WinNonlin 5.1. The PK dose (1.0 mg/kg i.v.) differs from the pharmacodynamic dose (20 nmol/kg i.p.); animals per group and blood sampling n for the PK arm are not reported in the body. BAY55-9837, DBAYL and Exendin-4 were stated to be 'used for control' in the half-life study but their individual doses and routes for that study are not reported. The BAY55-9837 half-life of ~5 min appears only in the Introduction as a previously reported value; no numeric BAY55-9837 or Exendin-4 half-life measured in this study is printed in the body (only fold-differences). The in vitro release assay used RPMI 1640 medium supplemented with 10% fetal bovine serum; this was a drug-release assay rather than a serum-stability assay, so it was classified as in vitro. Cmax and Tmax are not reported anywhere in the body.

- Curated ex vivo matrices: plasma|serum|tissue_organ.
- Bioanalytical method: Fluorescence of the Cy5.5 label in plasma read on an Odyssey Imaging System (Li-Cor) 700 nm channel against a standard curve of cyclized CTX:Cy5.5 in wild-type C57BL/6 plasma; RP-HPLC integration at 215 nm for the human-serum stability assay.
- Body-quoted half-lives: cyclic chlorotoxin:Cy5.5 (cyclized CTX:Cy5.5): 11 h [mouse serum/plasma after a single intravenous injection] ;; native (linear) chlorotoxin:Cy5.5 (CTX:Cy5.5): approximately 14 h [mouse serum (value stated as known in advance of the current studies, i.e. from prior work)].
- Caveats: Only cyclic CTX:Cy5.5 was pharmacokinetically profiled in this study (11 h, no error term). The ~14 h half-life for linear CTX:Cy5.5 is stated in the body as prior knowledge ('We knew, in advance of the current studies') rather than as data generated here, and no dose, group size or error term is given for it. No Cmax, tmax, AUC or clearance is reported for any agent. Doses are given as volume x molarity (50 uL of 40 uM) with no mg/kg equivalent. No amino-acid sequences appear in the body text; Fig. 1B is stated to contain them but the figure image is not available in this text file. The half-life measures the Cy5.5 fluorescent signal, not intact peptide.

- Curated in vivo routes: iv|not_administered.
- Bioanalytical method: LC/MS/MS with electrospray ionization; standard curves prepared in citrated mouse plasma (2000 to 4 nM) with TCEP added to enhance peptide recovery; acetonitrile protein precipitation.
- Body-quoted half-lives: RMP16 (recombinant slow-release TNF alpha-derived peptide): 13.100 ± 1.49 h (Table 2); stated as 13.1 h in the Results text [BALB/c mouse plasma] ;; P16 (TNF alpha segment, residues 75-94): 0.096 ± 0.008 h (Table 2); stated as 5.76 min in the Results text [BALB/c mouse plasma] ;; TNF alpha (recombinant human): 0.56 ± 0.13 h (Table 2); stated as 33.6 min in the Results text [BALB/c mouse plasma].
- Caveats: PK parameters come from Table 2 for all three agents at 5 mg/kg IV. The Table 2 'Units' column gives 'h' for t1/2 and the Methods explicitly name the fitted parameter 'elimination half-life (t1/2)' from a two-compartment elimination model in WinNonlin, so these are half-lives in hours, not rate constants, and half_life_phase is 'elimination'. Dosing was intravenous, so no absorption phase exists or is reported. Results text and Table 2 agree (0.096 h = 5.76 min; 0.56 h = 33.6 min). A separate in vitro Factor Xa digestion half-life for RMP16 is also reported - 'the factor Xa enzyme digestion half-life of RMP16 was significantly prolonged to 15.83 h from 9.72 h' in the presence of HSA - but this is a cell-free enzymatic stability value, not a plasma half-life, and is deliberately not placed in any agent half_life field. Cmax and tmax are not reported for any agent. The PK cohort strain is given only as 'BALB/c mice'; the tumour study used BALB/c-nu/nu nude mice. TNF alpha half-life figures quoted in the INTRO and DISCUSSION (15-30 min, ~30 min) are literature statements, not this study's measurements, so the Table 2 value is used. Role 'other' is not used here; H398 is recorded as a comparator because it is a pharmacological blocking agent tested alongside RMP16.

Half-lives are given as approximate values with no error terms and no named phase, so half_life_phase is 'unspecified' and both phase-specific fields are left 'not reported' - the paper does not fit or report a two-phase model. The authors caveat the KP-54 value: the RIA recognises all forms of kisspeptin, so the measured 32 min may partly reflect immunoreactive breakdown products; and they speculate that the apparent short half-life of KP-10 may reflect sequestration by serum proteins. Human KP-54 and KP-10 were used because the RIA does not detect mouse kisspeptins. The 3.8 min and 28 min human values quoted in the Discussion are literature comparisons, not measurements from this study, and are not recorded as agent values. Sequences are not printed in the body, so they are 'not reported' despite the PEPLIFE2 header listing them. BSA-FITC is a blood-brain-barrier tracer and dextrose-like challenge agents are not covered by the protocol role enum, so 'other' is used. Procedural agents (pentobarbital anaesthesia/euthanasia, paraformaldehyde perfusion) are not listed as tested substances.

Ward CW (2018) GsMTx4-D provides protection to the D2.mdx mouse. *Neuromuscul Disord*.
DOI: https://doi.org/10.1016/j.nmd.2018.07.005

- Contributes **1 records**, 1 unique sequences; 1 carry a numeric half-life.
- Recovered design: in vivo PK; PEPlife2 `Vivo/Vitro`: in vivo.
- Live animal dosed in 1 of 1 records.
- Assay matrices: plasma.
- Evidence available: **curated extraction** (PMC PMC6368066).
- Reported half-life: 168-168 h (median 168 h).
- PEPlife2 dose route: SC.
- Curated extraction: study types in vivo; 3 agents, 0 with a body-quoted half-life.
- Curated in vivo routes: ip|sc.
- Bioanalytical method: Liquid chromatography coupled with tandem mass spectrometry (LC-MS/MS), performed by Custom Biologics (Mississauga, Canada).
- Caveats: NO NUMERIC HALF-LIFE IN THE BODY. The '1-week half-life' appears only in the abstract ('GsMTx4-D showed a 1-week half-life when administered by subcutaneous injection over four weeks') and is therefore recorded as 'not reported in body' per the body-only rule. The body supports only qualitative washout statements (>50% decrease after one week; 12-19% of peak after two weeks; 'The long half-life of GsMTx4-D in tissues'). Peak tissue concentrations (~2 uM gastrocnemius and heart, ~8 uM diaphragm, >10-fold higher in liver and kidney) are steady-state accumulation levels from repeated dosing, not a single-dose Cmax, so cmax is left 'not reported'. The METHODS dosing list writes 'GsMTx4' where the rest of the paper writes 'GsMTx4-D' (e.g. '5 mice injected with 10 mg/kg GsMTx4, 3 times over 7 days'); these are taken to be the same administered agent. The statement that 'a single dose of 50 mg/kg subcutaneous dosing achieved ~1 uM concentration in the heart' is explicitly attributed to the authors' previous study in C57BL/6 mice, not to this work, and is not entered as a dose for this study. Note the Methods sacrifice schedule is internally inconsistent: three separate cohorts are each described as '6 times over 14 days' but sacrificed at 14, 21 and 28 days. The PEPLIFE2 header lists a 1 week half-life by LC-MS and a sequence for GsMTx4; both are database metadata and neither is supported by the article body.

- Curated in vivo routes: iv|not_administered|not_reported|other.
- Bioanalytical method: Gamma counting of 64Cu radioactivity in rat blood for the PK study; Western blot of 1:50 diluted mouse plasma with the F56-specific monoclonal antibody 9G10 and an albumin-specific antibody for the mouse albumin-conjugation time course; MALDI-TOF mass spectrometry for peptide-albumin conjugates.
- Body-quoted half-lives: 64Cu-DOTA-F56: 0.4249 h [rat blood] ;; 64Cu-DOTA-F56-CM: 6.967 h [rat blood].
- Caveats: CRITICAL SPECIES CAVEAT: the only numeric half-lives in this paper (0.4249 h for 64Cu-DOTA-F56 and 6.967 h for 64Cu-DOTA-F56-CM) were measured in female Sprague-Dawley RATS, not in mice. The body explicitly explains the choice: 'Because albumin has a half-life of 1.9 days in rats and 1.0 day in mice, we selected old female Sprague-Dawley rats... for the single-dose pharmacokinetic study.' The mouse experiment in the PK section was a qualitative Western blot of albumin conjugation, with no PK parameters. There is a dose discrepancy for F56-CM in mice: Methods give '5 mg/mL in PBS, injection volume, 10 mL/kg' while the Fig. 4A caption gives '5 mg/kg'; both are recorded verbatim and no arithmetic conversion was performed. A further internal inconsistency: Methods state the blood PK parameters 'were analyzed using a two-compartmental model' whereas Results attribute them to 'statistical moments analysis'; no phase label is attached to either half-life, so half_life_phase is 'unspecified' for both radiolabelled agents. Biodistribution %IA/g values at 4 h and 8 h in tumour-bearing nude mice are reported (Fig. 4E-F) but are not Cmax/AUC parameters and are not entered in the PK fields. Statements about albumin half-lives in mice, rats, rabbits and humans (1, 1.9, 5.55, 19 days) are cited background, not measurements from this study.

- Contributes **1 records**, 1 unique sequences; 1 carry a numeric half-life.

- Curated in vivo routes: ip|not_administered.
- Curated ex vivo matrices: not_reported|plasma.
- Bioanalytical method: not reported.

- Caveats: The supplied full-text file contains no METHODS or Experimental Section, so the bioanalytical assay, the exact PK dose and the PK sampling schedule are not recoverable from the body. The PEPLIFE2 header lists the assay as LC-MS/SIM; that is database metadata and was not used, so bioanalytical_method is recorded as not reported. The only PK datum in the body is the approximately 6 h plasma half-life of peptide 21 after intraperitoneal dosing. No phase is named, so half_life_phase is 'unspecified' and half_life_elimination is left as not reported rather than inferred. Dosing was intraperitoneal, so an absorption phase exists, but no absorption half-life and no rate constant is reported and nothing was converted. No Cmax, tmax or AUC is reported. The dose used specifically for the PK study is not stated; 20 mg/kg ip is the dose given for the HDL remodeling experiment in the immediately preceding sentence and 7.5 mg/kg ip was used for the anti-inflammatory study, so the PK dose is recorded as not separately stated. The eight-hour persistence of the pre-beta HDL effect is a pharmacodynamic duration, not a half-life. Over 80 cyclic d,l-alpha-peptides were screened (81 sequences per the Results text, with 73 eight-residue and 8 six-residue peptides); only the sequences printed in the body text or in Table 1 are captured as agent objects, and the remainder are listed only in Table S1, which is not part of the supplied body. Peptides 1, 15, 18, 23-26, 33, 34, 36-39, 41-50 and 52-54 are discussed by number in the Results without their sequences being printed. Lipopolysaccharide (LPS), given intraperitoneally to induce the acute inflammation model, is a model-induction agent and is noted here rather than listed as an agent object.

Plasma stability was 100% parent compound at 60 min (Table 1), with 94% +/- 0% (liver), 95% +/- 1% (kidney) and 95% +/- 1% (urine). Group-size conflict: Table 1 caption states n = 4, while the Methods state samples were taken at 5 min (n = 2) and 20 min (n = 2). The PK group size is internally inconsistent: 'Twelve ... mice ... divided into three groups (4 males and 4 females in each group)' cannot both be true (12 total would give 4 per group). The sentence reporting the ~4 h value is ambiguous in construction because it first praises compound 19 and then attributes the numeric half-life to compound 15; the value is recorded under 15 as written. Residue codes X1-X5 denote different sulfono-gamma-AA building blocks and KTail denotes the fatty-acid-conjugated lysine; the body does not expand these into standard one-letter codes. Human serum stability: analogue 19 showed no detectable degradation while glucagon showed >85% degradation over 24 h.

- Curated extraction: study types in vivo|in vitro; 7 agents, 1 with a body-quoted half-life.
- Curated in vivo routes: iv|not_administered.
- Bioanalytical method: Gamma counting of iodine-125 radioactivity (1480 Wizard gamma counter, Wallac Oy) in blood, brain and peripheral organs; sandwich ELISA for neprilysin and for Abeta40/Abeta42; fluorogenic substrate assay for neprilysin activity.

No Cmax, Tmax or AUC is reported; brain concentrations of 0.49±0.04, 0.42±0.04 and 0.11±0.01 %ID/g at 2, 6 and 24 h are biodistribution data, not a Cmax, and are recorded only in pk_quote. The '120 times longer' half-life claim and the 3 min half-life for SST alone are based on cited prior literature for SST, not on any measurement made in this paper; SST was deliberately not dosed in vivo here. The paper titles its biodistribution section 'Ex-vivo study', but the design is intact live mice dosed intravenously with organs harvested afterwards for radioactivity counting, so it is classified as in vivo rather than ex vivo under the protocol definition; no tissue was removed and then treated with the compound. The PEPLIFE2 header lists a long concatenated sequence for [125I]SST-scFv8D3; only the portions actually printed in the body (SST peptide, linker, His-tag) are recorded here.

## PMID 32778037

Beumer JH (2021) Mass Balance Study of the Engineered Cationic Antimicrobial Peptide, WLBU2, Following a Single Intravenous Dose of 14C-WLBU2 in Mice. *Curr Rev Clin Exp Pharmacol*.
DOI: https://doi.org/10.2174/1574884715666200810094201

- Contributes **1 records**, 1 unique sequences; 1 carry a numeric half-life.
- Recovered design: in vivo PK; PEPlife2 `Vivo/Vitro`: in vivo.
- Live animal dosed in 1 of 1 records.
- Assay matrices: plasma.
- Evidence available: **curated extraction** (PMC PMC8083974).
- Reported half-life: 22-22 h (median 22 h).
- PEPlife2 dose route: IV.
- Curated extraction: study types in vivo; 3 agents, 1 with a body-quoted half-life.
- Curated in vivo routes: iv.
- Bioanalytical method: Liquid scintillation counting of 14C (Wallac 1411 counter, BioSafe II scintillation fluid) on plasma, tissue and carcass homogenates, urine and faeces; plus HPLC-radiochromatography (Beckman Coulter System Gold, Phenomenex Luna C18(2) column, IN/US beta-RAM model-3 radio-HPLC detector, UV at 220 nm) for radioprofiling of plasma and tissue supernatants.
- Body-quoted half-lives: [14C]-WLBU2 ([(U)-14C-valine]-WLBU2 labelled at residues 4 and 7): approximately 22 h (plasma radioactivity) [mouse plasma (total 14C radioactivity, expressed as ug equivalents of WLBU2 per mL)].
- Caveats: IMPORTANT INTERPRETIVE CAVEAT stated by the authors: these are total-14C 'pseudo-pharmacokinetic' parameters, not parameters for intact WLBU2. The DISCUSSION states 'The pseudo-pharmacokinetic parameters of 14C content are a composite of the underlying individual parameters of the respective analytes with the 14C label. The 22 h terminal half-life of plasma radioactivity is, therefore, likely not the true terminal half-life, which would only be revealed with much longer sampling.' Roughly 34% of the dose was unaccounted for at 24 h and is attributed to expiration of 14CO2 after cleavage to the 14C-valine label. Parameters were derived by non-compartmental analysis from average concentration values using PK Solutions 2.0; only a terminal/elimination half-life is reported, and no absorption phase or absorption half-life is described (dosing was intravenous). Unit-header inconsistency in Table 2: the Cmax and AUC0-24 row labels carry per-gram units while the plasma column is per-mL; the parameter names and body text were used to assign plasma Cmax = 47.0±16.7 ug-eq/mL and plasma AUC0-24 = 266.6 ug-eq*h/g as printed. There is also an internal inconsistency on the single-dose MTD: the ABSTRACT says 20 mg/kg while the RESULTS body says 'The IV single dose MTD for WLBU2 is 30 mg/kg' and the DISCUSSION says 'The murine IV single dose, and daily x 5 MTD for WLBU2 were 30 and 15 mg/kg, respectively'; the body values are used here. Control plasma and control lung/liver/kidney homogenates were spiked with [14C]-WLBU2 or WLBU2 as bioanalytical recovery controls; these are method-validation steps rather than a biological ex vivo assay, so no ex vivo study type is claimed. An untreated (no injection) control group was included in the multiple-dose MTD study; it receives no substance and is therefore not listed as an agent.

## PMID 32512181

Martignoni M (2020) Degradation of thymic humoral factor γ2 in human, rat and mouse blood: An experimental and theoretical study. *Biochim Biophys Acta Proteins Proteom*.
DOI: https://doi.org/10.1016/j.bbapap.2020.140467

- Contributes **1 records**, 1 unique sequences; 1 carry a numeric half-life.
- Recovered design: ex vivo (fluid stability); PEPlife2 `Vivo/Vitro`: in vitro.
- Live animal dosed in 0 of 1 records.
- Assay matrices: blood.
- Evidence available: **curated extraction** (PMC PMC7274130).
- Reported half-life: 0.0933-0.0933 h (median 0.0933 h).
- PEPlife2 dose route: N.A..
- Curated extraction: study types ex vivo; 8 agents, 1 with a body-quoted half-life.
- Curated ex vivo matrices: blood|not_reported.
- Bioanalytical method: Radio-thin-layer chromatography: silica gel plates developed in water-methanol-acetic acid-chloroform (6:14:20:40 v/v), gel scraped in 1 cm sections and quantified by liquid scintillation counting (Soluene-350 / Hionic-Fluor), with unlabelled synthetic fragment peptides as Rf markers; samples first deproteinised with ice-cold acetonitrile and extracted with dichloromethane.
- Body-quoted half-lives: [3H]-THF-gamma2 (thymic humoral factor gamma2, thymoctonan), proline-labelled: 5.6 ± 0.2 min (mouse blood); 5.2 ± 0.4 min (human blood); 3.7 ± 0.2 min (rat blood) [whole heparinized blood, 37 C (mouse, human and rat)].
- Caveats: No animal or human was dosed in this study; the peptide was added to blood after collection, so this is entirely an ex vivo whole-blood degradation study and in_vivo_species is an empty array. ex_vivo_origin is recorded as 'human and animal' because both human donor blood and rodent blood were used; the protocol's single-value field cannot represent this. The PeplLife2 metadata labels the row 'In Vitro' and gives the assay as 'N.A.'; the actual assay described in the body is radio-TLC with liquid scintillation counting, not a chromatographic UV method. The PeplLife2 half-life of 5.6 +/- 0.2 min in mouse blood matches the body exactly. NOTE an internal inconsistency in the source: the Results section gives the human whole-blood half-life as 5.2 +/- 0.4 min, whereas the Discussion states 'a half-life of less than 4 min' for human whole blood; both figures are in the body and neither has been altered here. The ~12 min half-life in human plasma is cited from earlier work (Bramucci et al.), not measured here. There is also an apparent arithmetic inconsistency in the Methods between the stated 30 ng of labelled peptide added to 1 mL of blood and the stated final concentration of 93 nmol/mL; both values are quoted verbatim without correction. Rate constants for the rat/mouse model are given in the Fig. 6 caption (k1 = 0.15, k2 = 0.02, k3 = 0.08, k4 = 0.05, k5 = 0.025, k6 = 0.25, k7 = 0.03, k8 = 0.2, k9 = 0.0, k10 = 0.01 min^-1; A0 = 88.0). Mouse blood produced only two major fragments (2-8 and 2-6) versus three (2-8, 3-8, 2-6) in rat and human blood, and the authors conclude 'mouse would appear to be a less satisfactory model for the proteolysis in blood than the rat'.

The PK assay was fluorescence of the sulforhodamine-101 label on a microplate reader, NOT HPLC as the peplife2 metadata states; HPLC was used only for in vitro drug loading and GSH-triggered release. The PK/circulation study dosed intravenously via tail vein at 2 mg/kg, whereas the efficacy study used intraperitoneal dosing; the authors state there was no difference in biodistribution between routes (Figure S14). Sex of the BALB/c nude mice is not reported. The RGDDP targeting peptide sequence is given inconsistently in the body: 'CRGDKRGDSP' in the Introduction versus 'RGDSPRGDR' in the Results; the Results also describes RGDDP as consisting of the motifs RGDK and RGDSP. The efficacy Methods state 'AuNP-DPA, AuNP and DPA wre administered at dose of 2 mg/kg', while the Figure 7A caption lists '2 mg/kg DPA, 2 mg/kg AuNP-DPA or 2 mg/kg AuNP' and '1.5 mg/kg doxorubicin'. No Cmax, Tmax, AUC, clearance or volume of distribution values are reported anywhere in the body.

Tolerance findings: 50 mg/kg iv was lethal in mice within 1 min. Prior-publication PK values quoted in the Introduction for comparison (Onc72 and Onc112 at 1.8/5.4 mg/L ip and 5.7/8.4 mg/L iv; Api88 and Api137 at 1.0/0.4 mg/L iv and 0.6/0.2 mg/L ip after 5 mg/kg) are cited from earlier work, not measured here. Rat PK values are given in the Results text but the corresponding figures are in the Supplementary Material.

No absorption half-life is reported; the only absorption descriptor is Tmax = 10 min, so half_life_absorption is 'not reported' and the 10 min value is kept in tmax, not in half_life. No rate constants are printed, so no ln2/k conversion was needed or performed. Note the Methods say t1/2 'was calculated from C0 to C40' and that the Analysis section says a non-compartmental model (DAS 2.0) was used while the PK Methods say CL/F and Vd/F came from a first-order one-compartment model - this internal inconsistency is recorded but not resolved. Unit check: the Results text writes Cmax as '623 ng/mL' and the Discussion and Table 5 as '623 μg/L'; these are numerically identical, so there is no discrepancy. Separate in vitro value, deliberately NOT placed in the agent PK fields: intracellular TIPP in RBL-2H3 cells declined with 't1/2 = 5.596 min' after removal of extracellular peptide - that is a cellular efflux/degradation half-life, not a plasma half-life. Intravenous PK was attempted but not reported ('Due to rapid elimination after intravenous injection (data not shown)'). Anti-DNP-IgE (0.2 μg/mL), DNP-HSA (1 μg/mL), DTT, L-AA and PMSF were in vitro sensitising, challenge or stabiliser reagents rather than administered test substances and were not given agent objects.

The half-life therefore describes the triptolide payload of the cRGD-Exo/TP formulation rather than the cRGD peptide itself. Statistics are reported as mean ± SD. hUCMSCs were derived from human umbilical cords of healthy newborns with written informed consent, but no human subject was dosed, so no clinical arm is recorded. Zhang J (2023) Fast detection of liver fibrosis with collagen-binding single-nanometer iron oxide nanoparticles via T1-weighted MRI. *Proc Natl Acad Sci U S A*.

- Contributes **1 records**, 1 unique sequences; 1 carry a numeric half-life.
- Recovered design: in vivo PK; PEPlife2 `Vivo/Vitro`: in vivo.
- Live animal dosed in 1 of 1 records.
- Assay matrices: blood.
- Evidence available: **curated extraction** (PMC PMC10161015).
- Reported half-life: 0.095-0.095 h (median 0.095 h).
- PEPlife2 dose route: IV.
- Curated extraction: study types in vivo|ex vivo|in vitro; 5 agents, 1 with a body-quoted half-life.
- Curated in vivo routes: iv|not_administered|oral.
- Curated ex vivo matrices: blood|not_reported|serum|tissue_organ.
- Bioanalytical method: Dynamic T1-weighted MRI (3D FLASH, 4.7 T small-bore animal scanner) with contrast-to-noise-ratio quantification in Horos; 56Fe ICP-MS (45Sc internal standard) used for iron quantification of formulations and in the in vitro collagen-binding assay; enzymatic fluorometric L-amino acid assay for CBP content.
- Body-quoted half-lives: SNIO–CBP (single-nanometer iron oxide nanoparticle conjugated to type I collagen-binding peptide): 5.7 ± 1.9 min (blood elimination half-life) [mouse blood pool (heart), measured as MRI contrast-to-noise ratio in vivo].

The CBP amino-acid sequence is never printed in the body (only the PeplifeDB metadata header lists one, which per protocol cannot be used). CM-101 has no numeric PK in the body, only a qualitative statement of similarity. CCl4 and olive oil are listed as agents because they were administered, but they are disease-induction/vehicle-control treatments rather than test articles. The FBS incubation is recorded as ex vivo per protocol, though it assessed nonspecific protein binding rather than stability. Total of 35 mice studied.

## PMID 37835489

Wu X (2023) Near-Infrared Imaging of Colonic Adenomas In Vivo Using Orthotopic Human Organoids for Early Cancer Detection. *Cancers (Basel)*.
DOI: https://doi.org/10.3390/cancers15194795

- Contributes **1 records**, 1 unique sequences; 1 carry a numeric half-life.
- Recovered design: in vivo PK; PEPlife2 `Vivo/Vitro`: in vivo.
- Live animal dosed in 1 of 1 records.
- Assay matrices: serum.
- Evidence available: **curated extraction** (PMC PMC10571995).
- Reported half-life: 3.6-3.6 h (median 3.6 h).
- PEPlife2 dose route: IV.
- Curated extraction: study types in vivo|ex vivo|in vitro; 5 agents, 1 with a body-quoted half-life.
- Curated in vivo routes: iv|not_administered|other.
- Curated ex vivo matrices: not_reported|serum|tissue_organ.
- Bioanalytical method: Analytical RP-HPLC with peak-area quantification for serum stability; NIR fluorescence imaging for all in vivo and organ measurements, using a modified rigid small-animal endoscope (lambda-ex 785 nm) and a Pearl Trilogy imager (lambda-ex 785 nm, lambda-em 820 nm).
- Body-quoted half-lives: QQT*-IRDye800: 3.6 h (stability half-life in mouse serum, not a plasma elimination half-life) [serum obtained from a CPC;Apc mouse, incubated at 37 °C (ex vivo stability assay, RP-HPLC peak area)].
- Caveats: IMPORTANT CAVEAT ON THE HALF-LIFE. The only half-life in the body, 3.6 h, comes from incubating the peptide in serum harvested from a CPC;Apc mouse at 37 °C and following it by RP-HPLC over 24 h. It is a proteolytic stability half-life measured ex vivo, not a plasma elimination half-life from a dosed animal, so half_life_phase is 'unspecified' and both half_life_absorption and half_life_elimination are 'not reported'. The paper's own section heading 'In Vivo Serum Stability' is misleading relative to its Methods; the matrix field records the assay as it was actually performed. No plasma or blood concentrations were ever measured in this study, so there is no Cmax, tmax or AUC. The in vivo pharmacokinetics section is entirely imaging-based: 'Quantified intensities showed a peak T/B ratio at 1 h after injection of QQT*-IRDye800. The signal decreased to the baseline by ~24 h post-injection', and Figure 5a gives 'a mean (±SD) value of 2.2 ± 0.4 at 1 h post-injection with clearance by ~24 h'. That 1 h peak is a tumour-to-background fluorescence ratio maximum, not a plasma tmax, so it was deliberately not entered in the tmax field. Dose is reported only as a concentration and volume (200 μM, 200 μL); no body weight normalised dose is given. Human involvement was limited to donor-derived colonoid tissue (adenoma from a 46-year-old male and a 64-year-old male, normal colon from a 43-year-old female deceased donor) cultured in vitro; no human subject was dosed, so clinical_detail is null. Biodistribution at 1 h showed strong kidney signal consistent with renal clearance, with increased liver and lung intensity attributed to vasculature; no toxicity was seen on necropsy, hematology or chemistry at 48 h.

## PMID 37397495

Boldin R (2023) Design, in silico and pharmacological evaluation of a peptide inhibitor of BACE-1. *Front Pharmacol*.
DOI: https://doi.org/10.3389/fphar.2023.1184006

- Contributes **1 records**, 1 unique sequences; 1 carry a numeric half-life.
- Recovered design: in vivo PK; PEPlife2 `Vivo/Vitro`: in vivo.
- Live animal dosed in 1 of 1 records.
- Assay matrices: plasma.
- Evidence available: **curated extraction** (PMC PMC10313070).
- Reported half-life: 0.69-0.69 h (median 0.69 h).
- PEPlife2 dose route: IV.
- Curated extraction: study types in vivo|in vitro; 5 agents, 1 with a body-quoted half-life.
- Curated in vivo routes: iv|not_administered|not_reported.
- Bioanalytical method: LC-MS/MS: UPLC (Waters) with a Luna C18 column (5 μm, 150 × 4.6 mm), 70% methanol/0.05% formic acid isocratic elution, coupled to an ESI-Xevo TQ-S triple quadrupole in positive ionisation mode; six-point calibration curve (0.1-20 μg/mL plasma, 5-40 μg/mL organs) with 1/x2 weighted linear regression.
- Body-quoted half-lives: peptide SLAFVDVLN (BACE-1 inhibitor derived from Merluccius productus): 0.69 h (Table 4; described in the Results text as a short half-life of 0.7 h) [mouse plasma (healthy adult male Swiss mice, retro-orbital blood, EDTA)].
- Caveats: Full Table 4 PK set, quoted verbatim: t1/2 0.69 h; Cp0 2,595.96 μg/L; V 0.00154 (mg/kg)/(μg/L); CL 0.00154 (mg/kg)/(μg/L)/h; AUC 0-t 2,595.96 μg/L*h; AUC 0-inf 2,595.96 μg/L*h; AUMC 2,595.96 μg/L*h^2; MRT 1 h; Vss 0.00150 mg/kg/(μg/L). DATA-QUALITY FLAG: Cp0, AUC 0-t, AUC 0-inf and AUMC are all printed as the identical number 2,595.96 despite carrying four different units, which is almost certainly a transcription error in the published table; the values are recorded exactly as printed and no attempt was made to correct or recompute them. The AUC field therefore should be treated as unreliable. Because this was an intravenous bolus there is no absorption phase; no Cmax or tmax parameter is reported, so both are 'not reported'. Cp0 (2,595.96 μg/L) is a back-extrapolated concentration at time zero from the compartmental fit and was deliberately NOT entered as Cmax. The nearest body statement about a peak is in the Discussion: 'The peptide was found in the plasma at all analyzed times, with the highest concentration 5 min after the injection, and decreased over time, typical of an intravenous administration' - 5 min was simply the earliest post-dose sampling point, so it is not recorded as a tmax. half_life_phase is 'unspecified': the Methods say only that parameters were 'calculated in a compartmental analysis of plasma data after the intravenous bolus input' and the paper never names an alpha, beta, distribution or elimination phase, so no phase was inferred and both phase-specific half-life fields are 'not reported'. No rate constant is printed anywhere, so no ln2/k conversion was needed. SEPARATE IN SILICO VALUE, DELIBERATELY EXCLUDED: Table 1 ('Properties of the peptide estimated computationally') lists 'Half-life (h) 1.90', repeated in the Results as 'Moreover, it has a half-life of 1.9 h'. That is a ProtParam/ExPASy prediction, not a measurement, and was not used for any half-life field. The Discussion notes the measured value was 'similar to the value calculated by the computational model'. Tissue distribution from the dosed animals (spleen, liver, lung, kidney, brain; peptide absent from pancreas; brain peak at 2 h) is an in vivo biodistribution readout on harvested organ lysates and was not classified as ex vivo. Molecular docking, ProtParam and ToxinPred analyses are in silico and are not representable in the study_types vocabulary.

|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 4189 | Peptide | 0.69 Hours | Mouse plasma | In Vivo | in vivo PK |
